M_{n}^{2}(\theta)
, which has a variance of smaller order for
|\theta|\ll n^{-1/2}
. The reason for this is that each summand in
M_{n}^{1}(\theta)
is a multiple of an indicator with a factor independent of
X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}
and
\theta
, whereas the summands in
M_{n}^{2}(\theta)
are multiples of indicators where the factor scales (almost) linearly in
\theta
. It should be noted that this is the deeper reason behind the Poisson limit established in Theorem
1.1
and the non-validity of the Lindeberg condition of the classical martingale CLT (see Section
5
). The decomposition of
M_{n}(\theta)
now yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}}|M_{n}(\theta)|\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}}|M_{n}^{1}(\theta)|\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}}|M_{n}^{2}(\theta)|\right]

(4.18)

and we bound both terms on the right-hand side seperately (and with different techniques).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{M_{n}^{1}}
.

By direct evaluation (Appendix
G.2
), we have for some constant
C_{1}=C_{1}(\alpha,\beta)>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\leq C_{1}n|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|.

(4.19)

Note that this bound is the same as in (
G.5
). This is somewhat remarkable as
N_{n}^{L}
in (
G.5
) was given as a part of
\mathcal{I}_{5}(\theta)
which was the dominant one outside the
n^{-1/2}
-environment of
\rho_{0}
, but is no longer part of the dominant term
M_{n}^{1}
within the
n^{-1/2}
-environment. Defining the metric
\rho_{n}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})=\sqrt{C_{1}n|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|}
, by the same steps that were used to derive (
G.9
) and (
G.10
) we then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}}|M_{n}^{1}(\theta)|\right]&\leq\int_{1}^{\sqrt{C_{1}}2^{(j+1)/2}}\sqrt{C_{1}}2^{(j+1)/2}u^{-1}du\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\left(\sum_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}\cap\mathcal{T}_{k_{0}}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in U_{1/(C_{1}n)}(\theta)}\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(4.20)

where
\mathcal{T}_{k_{0}}^{n}
is the subset of the interval
[0,2^{j+1}/n]
that remains when stopping the procedure (
G.8
) for
\rho_{n}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\leq 1
. From the construction of the chaining sets it follows that
\mathcal{T}_{k_{0}}^{n}
contains at most
4C_{1}2^{j+1}
elements. The crucial observation making this chaining with a remainder term work is that we find a random variable
\overline{M}_{n}(\theta)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in U_{1/(C_{1}n)}(\theta)}\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}\leq\overline{M}_{n}^{1}(\theta)\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\overline{M}_{n}^{1}(\theta)^{2}\right]\leq C_{2}

(4.21)

for some
C_{2}=C_{2}(\alpha,\beta)>0
independent of
\theta
(see Appendix
G.2
for a detailed derivation). As the set
\overline{S}_{n,j}\cap\mathcal{T}_{n}
consists of less then
4C_{2}2^{j+1}
elements, we then conclude from (
4.20
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}}|M_{n}^{1}(\theta)|\right]\leq\sqrt{C_{1}}2^{(j+1)/2}\left(\log(\sqrt{C_{1}})+\frac{j+1}{2}\log(2)\right)+4\sqrt{C_{1}C_{2}}2^{(j+1)/2},

which is bounded from above by
C_{3}j2^{j/2}
for some constant
C_{3}=C_{3}(\alpha,\beta)>0
.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{M_{n}^{2}}
.

Because
M_{n}^{2}(\theta)-M_{n}^{2}(\theta^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}
with martingale increments
d_{k}
(meaning that
\mathbb{E}[d_{k}|X_{(k-1)/n}]=0
), Proposition
3.4
reveals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(M_{n}^{2}(\theta)-M_{n}^{2}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|^{2}n^{3/2}=:\tilde{\rho}_{n}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})^{2}

(4.22)

for some constant
C_{4}=C_{4}(\alpha,\beta)>0
. Denoting

D\left(u,\mathcal{T},\rho_{n}\right):=\max\{\#\mathcal{T}_{0}:\mathcal{T}_{0}\subset\mathcal{T},\rho_{n}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\geq u\textrm{ for different }\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{0}\}

the chaining step (
G.8
) for the function
\psi(x)=x^{2}
and metric
\tilde{\rho}_{n}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
given in (
4.22
) then reveals (analogously to (
G.9
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\overline{S}_{n,j}}|M_{n}^{2}(\theta)|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{C_{4}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}2^{j+1}}\left(D(u,\overline{S}_{n,j},\rho_{n})\right)^{1/2}du

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{C_{4}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}2^{j+1}}C_{4}^{1/4}2^{(j+1)/2}n^{-1/8}u^{-1/2}du=:C_{5}2^{j}n^{-1/4}.

In conclusion, (
4.17
) reveals

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}\left(\sup_{2^{M}<n\theta\leq K\sqrt{n}}\ell_{n}(\theta)\geq 0,A_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq M\\
2^{j}\leq\kappa_{0}\sqrt{n}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{C_{3}2^{j/2}j}{\zeta 2^{j}}+\frac{C_{5}2^{j}n^{-1/4}}{\zeta 2^{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq M\\
\kappa_{0}\sqrt{n}<2^{j}\leq K\sqrt{n}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{C_{3}2^{j/2}j\sqrt{n}}{\zeta 2^{2j}}+\frac{C_{5}2^{j}n^{1/4}}{\zeta 2^{2j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\leq\frac{C_{3}(1+\kappa_{0})}{\zeta\kappa_{0}}\sum_{j>M}j2^{-j/2}+\frac{C_{5}(1+\kappa_{0})}{\zeta\kappa_{0}}n^{-1/4}\log_{2}(K^{2}n).

As the series
\sum_{j\geq 0}j2^{-j/2}
is summable, the last term (and thus the right-hand side of (
4.16
)) tends to zero for
M\to\infty
and
n\to\infty
. The proof is then finished by (
4.15
).

5
Proof of Proposition
1.3

Before starting the proof, we want to draw attention to a particular feature of
M_{n}(z/n)
that suggests the Poissonian structure in the limit. It turns out that the only terms of
\ell_{n}(z/n)
contibuting to the variance of
M_{n}(z/n)
are parts of
\mathcal{I}_{2}(z/n)
and
\mathcal{I}_{8}(z/n)
(see Figure
3.1
), more precisely, they are given by the expressions

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z/n}}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z/n}}\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right)

(5.1)

and

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z/n}}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z/n}}\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right),

(5.2)

with
I_{2,k}^{z/n}
and
I_{8,k}^{z/n}
given in (
3.2
). Note that both of them are rare events. By orthogonality of martingale increments, one can show by means of Lemma
B.1
and Corollary
B.2
that the second moment of these expressions scales linearly in
z
with some bound independent of
n
. By Proposition
3.4
, all other terms in
M_{n}(z/n)
have second moments scaling as
z^{2}\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{n})
. Being sums of indicators over rare events, (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) indeed violate the Lindeberg condition of the classical martingale CLT if
L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)>0
, which can be seen as follows:
Setting

V_{nk}:=\log(\beta^{2}/\alpha^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z/n}},

we obtain with Lemma
B.1
and Corollary
B.2
that
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[V_{nk}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}]^{2}\rightarrow 0
in probability. Using this twice,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.(V_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[V_{nk}|X_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|V_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[V_{nk}|X_{(k-1)/n}]|>\epsilon\}}\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.V_{nk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|V_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[V_{nk}|X_{(k-1)/n}]|>\epsilon\}}\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.V_{nk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|V_{nk}|>\epsilon/2\}}\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq c_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

where the lower bound on the transition density in Lemma
B.1
has been used in the last inequality with the corresponding constant
c_{\alpha,\beta}
. Finally, this expression converges to some multiple of the local time
L_{T}^{\rho_{0}}(X)
by Lemma
3.2
.

By the Remark following Proposition
3.9
in
Häusler and
Luschgy (2015)
, the statement of the proposition follows if we verify

(i)

Stable convergence of fidis
: For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
z_{1},\dots,z_{N}\in\mathbb{R}
we have
\mathcal{F}
-stable convergence of the finite dimensional distributions, i.e.

\displaystyle\big(\ell_{n}(z_{1}/n),\dots,\ell_{n}(z_{N}/n)\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}-st}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ \big(\ell(z_{1}L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)),\dots,\ell(z_{N}L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X))\big).

(5.3)

(ii)

Tightness
: The process
(\ell_{n}(z/n))_{z\in[-K,K]}
is tight as an element of
\mathcal{D}([-K,K])
.

Part (ii) is proven using a moment criterion for tightness in the Skorohod space (
Billingsley (1999)
, Remark
(13.14)
after Theorem
13.5
) and fully given in Lemma
I.1
. Compared to many other situations, where the convergence of fidis is easier to derive, the harder part in our setting is to establish (i). By a stable version of the Cramér–Wold device (
Häusler and
Luschgy (2015)
, Corollary
3.19
(iii)), establishing (
5.3
) is equivalent to proving

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}\ell_{n}(z_{1}/n)+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell_{n}(z_{N}/n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}-st}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ \kappa_{1}\ell(z_{1}L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X))+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell(z_{N}L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X))

(5.4)

for any choice of
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{N}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Stable convergence of linear combinations in (
5.4
).
Proving (
5.4
) is the most involved part of the proof. In fact, we are going to derive a stable limit of

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{1}/n)+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{N}/n)

(5.5)

as stable convergence in the Skorohod space
\mathcal{D}([0,1])
. The reason for this is that arguments used for processes, in particular martingale arguments, can be applied. To the best of our knowledge, Theorem
2.1
in
Jacod (1997)
is the only result that deals with stable convergence in the setting of infill asymptotics and does not require a certain nestedness condition on the filtration (that is not valid in our setup). As the described result in
Jacod (1997)
only covers a continuous (in time) limit, we have to do some modification of Theorem
4.1
in
Jacod (2003)
that covers limit processes with jumps but does not allow in its current formulation to treat convergence of processes
X^{n}
, where each
X^{n}
is defined on a different stochastic basis
\mathcal{B}^{n}
. The resulting Proposition
H.1
is presented in Section
H
.
In order to properly present its application, we assume without loss of generality that
z_{m}<\dots<z_{1}<0<z_{m+1}<\dots<z_{N}
and define with
M=N-m

z_{0}=0,\qquad z_{j}^{-}:=z_{j},\ j=1,\dots,m\quad\textrm{ and }z_{j}^{+}:=z_{m+j},\ j=1,\dots,M.

Moreover, we denote the corresponding
\kappa_{i}
by
\kappa_{i}^{\pm}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\kappa_{i}\ell_{n,t}(z_{i}/n)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\kappa_{i}^{-}\ell_{n,t}(z_{i}^{-}/n)+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\kappa_{i}^{+}\ell_{n,t}(z_{i}^{+}/n).

With this notation, we are prepared to state the stable convergence result for the process (
5.5
) as follows:

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\mathbb{P})
be the standard Wiener space. Then there exists a very good extension
(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathcal{F}},(\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\overline{\mathbb{P}})
and a process
\overline{\ell}
on this extension such that the process

\kappa_{1}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{1}/n)+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{N}/n)

converges
\mathcal{F}
-stably to
\overline{\ell}
and the characteristics
(B,C,\nu)
of
(\overline{\ell},W)
are given by

B=\begin{pmatrix}b_{\alpha,\beta}^{\prime}L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\kappa_{i}^{-}|z_{i}^{-}|+b_{\alpha,\beta}L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\sum_{i=1}^{M}\kappa_{i}^{+}|z_{i}^{+}|\\
0\end{pmatrix},\qquad C=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathrm{id}_{[0,1]}\end{pmatrix},

where
\mathrm{id}_{[0,1]}:[0,1]\rightarrow[0,1]
denotes the identity, and

\displaystyle\nu(dt,dx,dy)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}dL_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\otimes\sum_{i=1}^{m}\delta_{\left(\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)(\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}),0\right)}(dx,dy)\left|z_{i}^{-}-z_{i-1}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}dL_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\otimes\sum_{i=1}^{M}\delta_{\left(\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)(\kappa_{i}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}),0\right)}(dx,dy)\left|z_{i}^{+}-z_{i-1}^{+}\right|.

The proof of this result is deferred to the next Subsection
5.1
.

Construction of the limit
\overline{\ell}
.

To explicitly construct the limiting process
\overline{\ell}
in Proposition
5.1
, it is necessary to capture its nature in both the variables
t
and
z
. To this aim, it is constructed based on two independent bivariate Poisson processes (instead of an univariate Poisson process that is sufficient to describe the limit of the MLE).
For the technical details, let

\Omega^{\prime\prime}=\big\{\mathbb{N}_{0}\textrm{-valued measure on }([0,1]\times\mathbb{R},\mathcal{B}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}))\big\}^{2},

\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}=\sigma\Big((\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}:\mu_{1}(B_{1})=k_{1},\mu_{2}(B_{2})=k_{2}:B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathcal{B}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}),k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Big)

with the process

N(t,z)=\begin{cases}\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[0,-z)),&\textrm{ if }z<0,\\
\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[0,z]),&\textrm{ if }z\geq 0.\end{cases}

Furthermore, set

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}=\bigcap_{t>s}\sigma(N(u,z):u\leq t,z\in\mathbb{R})

and define a measure
\hat{\mathbb{P}}(d\omega,d\omega^{\prime\prime})=\mathbb{P}(d\omega)Q_{\omega}(d\omega^{\prime\prime})
on
(\Omega\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})
such that for the identity process on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
the first component is a Poisson point process with intensity measure
\nu_{1}^{\omega}
given by

\nu_{1}^{\omega}([s,t],[z_{1},z_{2}]):=\big(L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)\big)|z_{1}-z_{2}|\alpha^{-2}

and the second component of the identity process is an independent Poisson point process with intensity measure
\nu_{2}^{\omega}
given by

\nu_{2}^{\omega}([s,t],[z_{1},z_{2}]):=\big(L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)\big)|z_{1}-z_{2}|\beta^{-2}.

Endowing
(\Omega\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\mathbb{P}})
with the filtration
(\bigcap_{t>s}\mathcal{F}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\prime\prime})_{s\in[0,1]}
, the resulting stochastic basis is a very good extension of
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{s})_{s\in[0,1]},\mathbb{P})
, because the mapping
\omega\mapsto Q_{\omega}(N(u,z)=k)=|z|L_{u}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)
is
\mathcal{F}_{u}
-measurable,
L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)
is continuous and
(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime})_{s\in[0,1]}
is right-continuous.
Building on these definitions, we set

\displaystyle\overline{\ell}(t,z)

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\geq 0\}}\left(b_{\alpha,\beta}-\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right)+\mathbbm{1}_{\{z<0\}}\left(b_{\alpha,\beta}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)\right)|z|L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\geq 0\}}\log(\beta^{2}/\alpha^{2})N(t,z)+\mathbbm{1}_{\{z<0\}}\log(\alpha^{2}/\beta^{2})N(t,z)\right).

In what follows, we prove the characteristics of

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\overline{\ell}(\bullet,z_{j}^{-})+\sum_{j=1}^{M}\kappa_{j}^{+}\overline{\ell}(\bullet,z_{j}^{+}),W\right)

(5.6)

to be equal to the characteristics given in Proposition
5.1
. As they are clearly
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-predictable, Theorem
3.2
in
Jacod (2003)
reveals that the
\mathcal{F}
-conditional law of the processes
\ell
in Proposition
5.1
and the first coordinate of (
5.6
) are the same.

In order to derive the characteristics of (
5.6
), we need a preliminary result. Here, we slightly abuse notation and passagewise explicitly highlight the dependence of random variables on their respective argument
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
within conditional expectation.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let

\hat{\Omega}=\Omega\times\Omega^{\prime},\quad\hat{\mathcal{F}}=\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\quad\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}=\bigcap_{s>t}\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime},\quad\hat{\mathbb{P}}(d\omega,d\omega^{\prime})=\mathbb{P}(d\omega)Q_{\omega}(d\omega^{\prime})

be a very good extension of
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\mathbb{P})
in the sense of Definition
II.7.1
in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
. In particular, for any
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\prime}
, the map
\omega\mapsto Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable. Let
X:\Omega\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a random variables in
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
. Then for any
s\in[0,1]
,

\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[X|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[X|\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]\quad a.s.

and for
Y_{s}:\Omega^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
being in
L^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
and independent of
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime}
,

\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[Y_{s}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}\left[Y_{s}\right]\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]\quad a.s.

Let
A\in\mathcal{F}_{s}
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime}
. Then

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[X|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\right]=\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{A}X|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{A}X|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{A}Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})X|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A}Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})X\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbbm{1}_{A^{\prime}}X]\right]=\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}X\right].

Here, the third step follows as
Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
measurable. Now the first claim follows from the definition of conditional expectation and
\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime}=\sigma(A\times A^{\prime}:A\in\mathcal{F}_{s},A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime})
.

For the second claim, let
A,A^{\prime}
be as above. Then,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[Y_{s}]|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[Y_{s}]|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\right]\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{A}Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[Y_{s}]|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{A}Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[Y_{s}]|\mathcal{F}_{s}]\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{A}Q_{\omega}(A^{\prime})\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[Y_{s}]\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[\mathbbm{1}_{A^{\prime}}Y_{s}]\big]

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A\times A^{\prime}}Y_{s}\right]

and the second claim follows.
∎

We now derive the characteristics of the process in (
5.6
) and decompose

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\kappa_{i}^{-}\overline{\ell}(\bullet,z_{i}^{-})+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\kappa_{i}^{+}\overline{\ell}(\bullet,z_{i}^{+})\\
W\end{pmatrix}=B+M,

where
M=(M_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
is given by

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
W\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\left(N(\bullet,z_{i}^{-})-|z_{i}^{-}|L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)/\alpha^{2}\right)\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\begin{pmatrix}\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{M}\kappa_{j}^{+}\left(N(\bullet,z_{i}^{+})-|z_{i}^{+}|L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)/\beta^{2}\right)\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

We establish the statement for each characteristic seperately:

\boldsymbol{B}
:

First, note that as
L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)
is continuous,
B
is predictable. Then, the statement about the first characteristic
B
follows if we can show that
M
is a
\hat{\mathbb{P}}
-martingale with respect to the filtration
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
. We prove this for
m=0
,
M=1
, the general case follows easily by linearity of conditional expectation. We have for
0\leq s\leq t\leq 1
, using Lemma
5.2

\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\begin{pmatrix}0\\
W_{t}\end{pmatrix}\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\begin{pmatrix}0\\
W_{t}\end{pmatrix}\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
W_{s}\end{pmatrix}

and consequently

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[M_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
W_{s}\end{pmatrix}+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\kappa_{1}^{+}\left(N(t,z_{1}^{+})-z_{1}^{+}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)/\beta^{2}\right)\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

To proceed, we use that by Lemma
5.2
,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.z_{1}^{+}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)/\beta^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\left.z_{1}^{+}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)/\beta^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\Big].

As
N(s,z_{1}^{+})
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}
-measurable and
N(t,z_{1}^{+})-N(s,z_{1}^{+})=\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times[0,z_{1}^{+}])
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}
, Lemma
5.2
reveals

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[N(t,z_{1}^{+})|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[N(t,z_{1}^{+})-N(s,z_{1}^{+})|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]+\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[N(s,z_{1}^{+})|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[N(t,z_{1}^{+})-N(s,z_{1}^{+})]\big|\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]+N(s,z_{1}^{+}).

Here and in what follows, by slight abuse of notation, we explicitly indicate the dependence of
Q_{\omega}
on
\omega
to highlight that the conditional expectation affects the corresponding expression. Recalling that the second component
\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
of the identity on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
is a Poisson point process with intensity measure
\nu_{2}^{\omega}([s,t]\times[z_{1},z_{2}])=\left(L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\omega)\right)|z_{1}-z_{2}|/\beta^{2}
under
Q_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[N(t,z_{1}^{+})-N(s,z_{1}^{+})]=\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\omega}}[\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times[0,z_{1}^{+}])]=\left(L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right)z_{1}^{+}/\beta^{2}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\kappa_{1}^{+}\left(N(t,z_{1}^{+})-z_{1}^{+}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)/\beta^{2}\right)\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\kappa_{1}^{+}N(s,z_{1}^{+})+\kappa_{1}^{+}\left(L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right)z_{1}^{+}/\beta^{2}-\kappa_{1}^{+}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)z_{1}^{+}/\beta^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\kappa_{1}^{+}N(s,z_{1}^{+})-\kappa_{1}^{+}L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)z_{1}^{+}/\beta^{2}

which yields

\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[M_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}^{\prime\prime}\right]=M_{s}\quad a.s.

Let
(s_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a decreasing sequence with
s_{n}\searrow s
. Then by right-continuity of
M
and the tower property of conditional expectation, together with dominated convergece that is applicable by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality as
M
is square-integrable,

\hat{\mathbb{E}}[M_{t}|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}]=\hat{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\mathbb{E}}[M_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{s_{n}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s_{n}}^{\prime\prime}]\big|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\big]=\hat{\mathbb{E}}\big[M_{s_{n}}|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\big]\longrightarrow\hat{\mathbb{E}}[M_{s}|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}]=M_{s}.

\boldsymbol{C}
:

The continuous martingale part of
M
is
M^{c}=(0,W)^{t}
. Hence, it is clear that

C=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathrm{id}_{[0,1]}\end{pmatrix}.

\boldsymbol{\nu}
:

The third characteristic
\nu
is given as the predictable compensator of the jump measure associated with the process
(Z,W)
, where
Z_{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\kappa_{i}^{-}\overline{\ell}(s,z_{i}^{-})+\sum_{i=1}^{M}\kappa_{i}^{+}\overline{\ell}(s,z_{i}^{+})
, i.e. of

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq 0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta((Z,W)_{s})\neq(0,0)\}}\delta_{(s,\Delta Z_{s},\Delta W_{s})}=\sum_{s\geq 0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta Z_{s}\neq 0\}}\delta_{(s,\Delta Z_{s},0)}.

To proceed, we first note that
\Delta Z_{s}=\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Delta N(s,z_{i}^{-})+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{M}\Delta N(s,z_{i}^{+})
. Morover, we observe:

(a)

For
i<j
, we have
z_{i}^{-}>z_{j}^{-}
and thus

N(t,z_{j}^{-})=\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[0,-z_{j}^{-}))=\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[0,-z_{i}^{-}))+\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[-z_{i}^{-},-z_{j}^{-})).

From this, it follows that
N(\bullet,-z_{j}^{-})
jumps every time the process
N(\bullet,-z_{i}^{-})
jumps.

(b)

Analogously, for
i<j
, we have
z_{i}^{+}<z_{j}^{+}
and thus

N(t,z_{j}^{+})=\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[0,z_{j}^{+}])=\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[0,z_{i}^{+}])+\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}([0,t]\times[z_{i}^{+},z_{j}^{+}]).

From this, it follows that
N(\bullet,z_{j}^{+})
jumps every time the process
N(\bullet,z_{i}^{+})
jumps.

From these considerations and the fact that
\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}([0,\bullet]\times[0,-z])
and
\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}([0,\bullet]\times[0,z^{\prime}])
jump at the same time with probability zero (due to their independence), it follows that

\displaystyle(g\star\hat{\mu})_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\leq t}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta Z_{u}=\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\}}g\left(\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right),0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{u\leq t}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta Z_{u}=\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{i}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\}}g\left(\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{i}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right),0\right).

By continuity of
L_{\bullet}^{\rho_{0}}(X)
,
(g\star\nu)
is predictable with respect to
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s})_{s\in[0,1]}
. It remains to show that
(g\star\hat{\mu})_{\bullet}-(g\star\nu)_{\bullet}
is an
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s})_{s\in[0,1]}
-martingale. From (a) and (b) above, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{s<u\leq t}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta Z_{u}=\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s<u\leq t}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta\left(\kappa_{1}^{-}N(\bullet,z_{1}^{-})+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}N(\bullet,z_{m}^{-})\right)_{u}=\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\}}

\displaystyle=\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times[0,-z_{i}^{-}))-\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times[0,-z_{i-1}^{-}))

\displaystyle=\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times[-z_{i-1}^{-},-z_{i}^{-})).

Analogously,

\sum_{s<u\leq t}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Delta Z_{u}=\kappa_{i}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\}}=\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times(z_{i-1}^{+},z_{i}^{+}]).

Then,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[(g\star\hat{\mu})_{t}-(g\star\nu)_{t}-(g\star\hat{\mu})_{s}+(g\star\nu)_{s}\Big|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\sum_{i=1}^{m}g\left(\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right),0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\cdot\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times[-z_{i-1}^{-},-z_{i}^{-}))-\frac{L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)}{\alpha^{2}}|z_{i}^{-}-z_{i-1}^{-}|\right|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{i=1}^{M}g\left(\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{i}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right),0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\cdot\hat{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.\omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}((s,t]\times(z_{i-1}^{+},z_{i}^{+}])-\frac{L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)-L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)}{\beta^{2}}|z_{i}^{+}-z_{i-1}^{+}|\right|\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\right]

and by the same arguments used for
B
, both conditional expectations vanish.

Finalizing the proof of (
5.4
).

From Proposition
5.1
and the explicit construction of
\overline{\ell}
in the preceding step, we obtain

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{1}/n)+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{N}/n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}-st}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ \kappa_{1}\overline{\ell}(\bullet,z_{1})+\dots+\kappa_{N}\overline{\ell}(\bullet,z_{N}).

(5.7)

as stable convergence in the Skorohod space
\mathcal{D}([0,1])
. Then, (
5.4
) follows from this by using that the projection onto the endpoint
1
is continuous in
\mathcal{D}([0,1])
, combined with a stable version of the continuous mapping theorem (
Häusler and
Luschgy (2015)
, Theorem
3.18
(c)) and the fact that

\left(L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X),\left(\overline{\ell}(1,z)\right)_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{=}}\left(L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X),\left(\ell(zL_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X))\right)_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\right).

∎

5.1
Proof of Proposition
5.1

Recall that

\kappa_{1}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{1}/n)+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell_{n,\bullet}(z_{N}/n)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa_{j}B_{n,\bullet}(z_{j}/n)+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa_{j}M_{n,\bullet}(z_{j}/n),

which is the canonical semimartingale decomposition of the process on the left-hand side with respect to the filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{n})_{t\in[0,1]}
with
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{n}:=\mathcal{F}_{\lfloor nt\rfloor/n}
on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. Furthermore, note that

\kappa_{1}\ell_{n,t}(z_{1}/n)+\dots+\kappa_{N}\ell_{n,t}(z_{N}/n)=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}y_{nk}

for the random variables

y_{nk}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa_{j}\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+z_{j}/n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right).

In order to apply Proposition
H.1
we have to establish the following for every
t\in[0,1]
:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq t}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa_{j}B_{n,s}(z_{j}/n)-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\kappa_{j}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{j}\geq 0\}}b_{\alpha,\beta}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{j}<0\}}b_{\alpha,\beta}^{\prime}\right)|z_{j}|L_{s}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}0,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[(y_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{i}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)^{2}\left|z_{i}^{-}-z_{i-1}^{-}\right|\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left(\kappa_{i}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)^{2}\left|z_{i}^{+}-z_{i-1}^{+}\right|,\end{split}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[(y_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}\ 0,

(5.10)

and for each
g:\mathbb{R}^{2}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
measurable, non-negative, bounded, Lipschitz-continuous and vanishing in a neighborhood of zero,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.g\left(y_{nk},W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right)\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}\ (g\star\nu)_{t}.

(5.11)

Finally, we also show for suitable
\kappa>0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}y_{nk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y_{nk}|>\kappa\}}\right]\longrightarrow\ 0.

(5.12)

Condition (
5.8
) follows from Corollary
3.3
and conditions (
5.9
), (
5.10
) and (
5.12
) are proven in Appendix
I
. We are going to show the most interesting one, namely (
5.11
) on the jump characteristic that brings out the bivariate Poissonion nature of the limit
\overline{\ell}
. To this aim, it is necessary to separate the terms that contribute to the jumps of the process by rewriting

\displaystyle y_{nk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\left(-\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)+\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)\right).

Let
\delta>0
be chosen such that
g(x)=0
for
\|x\|_{2}<\delta
which is possible since
g
vanishes in a neighborhood of zero. Furthermore, we define

J_{k}:=\bigcup_{l=1}^{m}\left(I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}\cup I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}\right)\cup\bigcup_{l=1}^{M}\left(I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}\cup I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}\right)

which corresponds to those cases for
X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}
that contribute to the variance of at least one of
\ell_{n}(z_{m}^{-}/n),\dots,\ell_{n}(z_{M}^{+}/n)
. All other cases then are summarized in
J_{k}^{c}
and we rewrite with
w_{nk}=W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].\end{split}

(5.13)

It will turn out that the first summand is the contributing term whereas the second one is of smaller order which is discussed in the following: First, by our choice of
\delta
and the boundedness assumption on
g
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|(y_{nk},w_{nk})\|_{2}\leq\delta\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|(y_{nk},w_{nk})\|_{2}>\delta\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|g\|_{\textrm{sup}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|(y_{nk},w_{nk})\|_{2}>\delta\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

For the rest of the proof, we will denote
C=C(\alpha,\beta,m,M,z_{m}^{-},\dots,z_{M}^{+},\kappa_{m}^{+},\dots,\kappa_{M}^{+})
for a constant that depends on both
\alpha,\beta
and the parameters of the linear combination
\kappa_{1}\ell_{n,t}(z_{1}/n)+\dots,\kappa_{N}\ell_{n,t}(z_{N}/n)
. By Proposition
3.4
we then find the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|(y_{nk},w_{nk})\|_{2}>\delta\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right]\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}^{c}}\|(y_{nk},w_{nk})\|_{2}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(|w_{nk}|^{2}+\sum_{j=1,3,4,5,6,7,9}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)\right)^{2}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{j=1,3,4,5,6,7,9}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}|\kappa_{l}^{-}|^{4}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|^{4}\right]+\sum_{l=1}^{M}|\kappa_{l}^{+}|^{4}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|^{4}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{C}{\delta^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|w_{nk}|^{4}\right]\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{4}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\right)\longrightarrow 0.

It remains to study the first summand on the right-hand side of (
5.13
) that is further split as

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},w_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},0)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(g(y_{nk},w_{nk})-g(y_{nk},0)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

By Lemma
B.1
and Corollary
B.2
, we exemplarily obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{-}/n}}\right]\leq C\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\int_{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n}^{\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C\frac{|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-z_{l-1}^{-}/n)^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right]\leq C\frac{|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|}{\sqrt{nk}},\end{split}

and by similar estimations, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(z_{m}^{-},\dots,z_{M}^{+})\frac{1}{\sqrt{nk}}.

(5.14)

From this, the boundedness and Lipschitz assumption on
g
(where
L_{g}
denotes the Lipschitz constant of
g
) and Markov’s inequality, it follows for any
\epsilon>0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(g(y_{nk},w_{nk})-g(y_{nk},0)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\epsilon\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}\right]+\|g\|_{\sup}\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}\left(|w_{nk}|>\epsilon/L_{g}\right)\leq C\epsilon+L_{g}^{4}\epsilon^{-4}\frac{nt}{n^{2}}.

Thus,

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(g(y_{nk},w_{nk})-g(y_{nk},0)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]=o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

and it suffices to investigate the limit in probability of

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[g(y_{nk},0)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

For this study, we abbreviate
\tilde{g}(x):=g(x,0)
and note that
\tilde{g}
is bounded, Lipschitz-continuous with Lipschitz constant
L_{g}
and vanishes in a neighborhood of zero (by our assumption on
g
). Suitably rearranging the indicators in the definition of
J_{k}
into indicators of disjoint sets yields

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{i}^{+}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{i+1}^{+}/n\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{i}^{+}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{i+1}^{+}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right),

and we obtain a corresponding decomposition

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\tilde{g}(y_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4},

(5.15)

where

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(y_{nk}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(y_{nk}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(y_{nk}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{i}^{+}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{i+1}^{+}/n\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(y_{nk}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{i}^{+}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{i+1}^{+}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

In the following, we are going to evaluate these four summands seperately.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{T_{1}}.

With
A_{kl}=\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}
we have

A_{kl}\cap I_{j,k}^{0,z_{u}^{+}/n}=\emptyset\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad A_{kl}\cap I_{8,k}^{z_{v}^{-}/n,0}=\emptyset

for all
j=2,8
,
1\leq l,v\leq m
,
1\leq u\leq M
and
1\leq k\leq n
, and obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{g}\left(y_{nk}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\tilde{g}\left(\sum_{u=1}^{m}\kappa_{u}^{-}\left(-\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{u}^{-}/n,0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{u}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left.+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\kappa_{u}^{+}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{u}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{u}^{+}/n}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\tilde{g}\left(\sum_{u=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\sum_{u=l}^{m}\kappa_{u}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}-\sum_{u=l}^{m}\sum_{v=u}^{m}\kappa_{v}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{v}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\Big)\Bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle T_{1}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]+r_{1,n},

where for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{1,n}|\right]\leq\epsilon\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right]+2\|g\|_{\sup}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}\left(\left|\sum_{u=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\left.\left.+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|+\sum_{u=l}^{m}\sum_{v=u}^{m}|\kappa_{v}^{-}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}>\frac{\epsilon}{L_{g}}\right).

By the same estimation as used for (
5.14
), we obtain by Lemma
B.1
and Corollary
B.2

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right]\leq\frac{C}{n\sqrt{k}}.

Then, by Markov’s inequality together with Proposition
3.4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}\left(\left|\sum_{u=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.+\sum_{u=l}^{m}\sum_{v=u}^{m}|\kappa_{v}^{-}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}>\frac{\epsilon}{L_{g}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{2L_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left|\sum_{u=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{2L_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{u=l}^{m}\sum_{v=u}^{m}|\kappa_{v}^{-}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{CL_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\sum_{u=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)|^{2}\right]+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)|^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{CL_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{u=l}^{m}\sum_{v=u}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{CL_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}

such that with (
5.14
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{1,n}|\right]&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\frac{C\epsilon}{\sqrt{nk}}+\frac{CL_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}\right)\leq C\epsilon+\frac{CL_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{nt}}{n}.\end{split}

(5.16)

Note that
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[|r_{1,n}|]\leq C\epsilon
and as
\epsilon>0
was chosen arbitrary, this implies
r_{1,n}=o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)
. Next, by directly inserting the transition density for the corresponding regime,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|}{n\sqrt{2\pi/n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|}{n\sqrt{2\pi/n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1),

where the first step follows by a Taylor expansion and is given in detail for the treatment of
S_{31}(k)
within the verification of (
5.9
) in Appendix
I
and the second one follows as

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\right|\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\right]\longrightarrow 0,

which is shown analogously to
r_{n}^{7,2}
in the proof of Proposition
3.1
. Then, with (
5.16
),

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{m}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{T_{2}}.

With
A_{kl}=\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)/n}\}
we obtain

A_{kl}\cap I_{j,k}^{0,z_{u}^{+}/n}=\emptyset,\qquad A_{kl}\cap I_{2,k}^{z_{v}^{-}/n,0}=\emptyset,\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad A_{kl}\cap I_{8,k}^{z_{w}^{-}/n,0}=\emptyset

for all
j=2,8
,
1\leq l,v\leq m
,
1\leq u\leq M
,
w\leq l-1
and
1\leq k\leq n
. Then,

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{2}&=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(y_{nk}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\tilde{g}\left(\sum_{u=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\sum_{u=l}^{m}\kappa_{u}^{-}\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right.\right.\\
&\hskip 99.58464pt\left.\left.\left.+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]+r_{2,n},\end{split}

(5.17)

where for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{2,n}|\right]\leq\epsilon\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}\left(\left|\sum_{u=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{-}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{u}^{-}/n,0)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}+\sum_{u=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{u}^{+}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{u}^{+}/n)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}\right|>\frac{\epsilon}{L_{g}}\right).

By the same arguments used for
r_{1,n}
in the treatment of
T_{1}
before, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{2,n}|\right]&\leq C\epsilon+\frac{CL_{g}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{nt}}{n}\end{split}

(5.18)

and consequently
r_{2,n}=o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)
. Next,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A_{kl}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|}{n\sqrt{2\pi/n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\beta^{2}/n}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1),

as was already proven in the treatment of
S_{32}
in the proof of (
5.9
). Combining this with (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) then gives

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\beta^{2}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{m}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{T_{3}}.

By similar arguments as used for
T_{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{M}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}|+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{T_{4}}.

By similar arguments as used for
T_{1}
we obtain

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\beta^{2}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{M}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}|+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1).

Recalling the definition (
3.7
) for
\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}
, summing up our expansions and then applying Lemma
3.2
finally gives with (
5.15
)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\tilde{g}(y_{nk})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|\frac{1}{n}\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq\lfloor nt\rfloor}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}|\frac{1}{n}\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq\lfloor nt\rfloor}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+L_{t}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\tilde{g}\left(\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right)|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}|.

This supplementary material is organized as follows:

Contents

A
Notation

Throughout the whole technical supplement,
C_{\alpha,\beta}
denotes a real and positive constant that only depends on
\alpha
and
\beta
but may change from line to line. Any other dependencies are highlighed explicitly, for example by writing
C_{\alpha,\beta}(K)
in case the constant additionally depends on
K
.
We frequently use the notation

\mathbb{P}(A,B):=\mathbb{P}(A\cap B).

We denote the four regimes of the transition density (
3.1
) with
P_{i}^{\rho}(x,y;t)
,
i=1,2,3,4
, i.e.

\displaystyle P_{1}^{\rho}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}\alpha}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{2t\alpha^{2}}\right)-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-2\rho+x)^{2}}{2t\alpha^{2}}\right)\right],

\displaystyle P_{2}^{\rho}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}\beta}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{2t\beta^{2}}\right)+\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-2\rho+x)^{2}}{2t\beta^{2}}\right)\right],

\displaystyle P_{3}^{\rho}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2t}\left(\frac{y-\rho}{\beta}-\frac{x-\rho}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\right),

\displaystyle P_{4}^{\rho}(x,y;t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2t}\left(\frac{y-\rho}{\alpha}-\frac{x-\rho}{\beta}\right)^{2}\right).

Note that in constrast to
p_{t}^{\rho}(x,y)
, each
P_{i}^{\rho}(x,y;t)
is continuous (and differentiable) in the parameter
\rho
.

B
Preliminary results

Lemma B.1
.

The transition density (
3.1
) is dominated by a Gaussian density, i.e.

p_{t}^{\rho}(x,y)\leq\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\frac{\max\{\alpha,\beta\}}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{2t\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}\right).

Moreover,

p_{t}^{\rho}(x,y)\geq\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\frac{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}{\max\{\alpha,\beta\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{2t\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}\right).

This is straightforward and done by a case-by-case study for the four regimes.
∎

Corollary B.2
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{R}
,
b\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
l<k
. Then there exists a constant
c_{\alpha,\beta}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-a)^{2}}{2b}\right)\right|X_{l/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq c_{\alpha,\beta}\sqrt{\frac{b}{b+(k-l)\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{(a-x_{0})^{2}}{b+(k-l)\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right).

In particular, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-a)^{2}}{2b/n}\right)\right|X_{l/n}\right]\leq c_{\alpha,\beta}(b)\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-l}}

for some constant
c_{\alpha,\beta}(b)>0
not depending on
n
and
a
.

By Lemma
B.1
, the left-hand side of the statement is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(k-l)/n}}\frac{\max\{\alpha,\beta\}}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\frac{(z-a)^{2}}{2b}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{(z-x_{0})^{2}}{2(k-l)\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dz.

Set
d:=(k-l)\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n
. For the integral, we find by completing the square,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\frac{(z-a)^{2}}{2b}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{(z-x_{0})^{2}}{2d}\right)dz

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\exp\left(-\frac{a^{2}d+x_{0}^{2}b}{2bd}+\left(\frac{ad+x_{0}b}{b+d}\right)^{2}\frac{b+d}{2bd}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\frac{b+d}{2bd}\left(z-\frac{ad+x_{0}b}{b+d}\right)^{2}\right)dz

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{(a-x_{0})^{2}}{b+d}\right)\sqrt{2\pi\frac{bd}{b+d}}.

Then, the desired upper bound follows for the constant

c_{\alpha,\beta}=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}.

∎

Corollary
B.2
is often used in combination with the inequalities

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq 2\sqrt{n},\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k(k-l)}}\leq 4n.

Both of them are easily obtained by comparison with corresponding integral and applied without further notice.

Within the next lemma and its proof, we use the notation
a\wedge b:=\min\{a,b\}
for real numbers
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
f_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
be functions such that for every
c>0
we have the bound
|f_{n}(x,y)|^{m}\exp(-n(x-y)^{2}/c)\leq C(m,c)
for some constant
C=C(m,c)>0
, where
m\in\{1,2\}
and
C
being independent of
n
. Moreover, let
J_{1},J_{2}\subset\mathbb{R}
be two intervals (possibly infinite). Then for some constant
C(\alpha,\beta,c)>0
that depends only on
\alpha,\beta
and
c
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq C(\alpha,\beta,c)n\left(1\wedge\lambda(J_{1})\wedge\lambda(J_{2})\right)+C(\alpha,\beta,c)n^{2}\left(1\wedge\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leq C(\alpha,\beta,c)n\left(1\wedge\lambda(J_{1})\wedge\lambda(J_{2})\right)+C(\alpha,\beta,c)n^{2}\left(1\wedge\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}\right).

We will repeatedly use that for
l<k
and
i=1,2
by Lemma
B.1

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{i}}(X_{k/n})\mid X_{l/n}\right]&\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\int_{J_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{l/n})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta\}(k-l)/n}\right)dy\\
&\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{i})}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\right).\end{split}

(B.1)

Moreover, for every interval
J_{i}
, there exist constants
c_{1}(J_{i}),c_{2}(J_{i})
(the endpoints of the interval, possibly
\pm\infty
) such that for any
d>0
and with the shorthand notation

\nu(J_{i},X_{(k-1)/n}):=\min\{(X_{(k-1)/n}-c_{1}(J_{i}))^{2},(X_{(k-1)/n}-c_{2}(J_{i}))^{2}\}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{J_{i}}\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{d/n}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\sqrt{\pi d}\wedge\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{i}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\sqrt{\pi d}+\mathbbm{1}_{J_{i}^{c}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\lambda(J_{i})\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{i},X_{(k-1)/n})}{d/n}\right)\right).\end{split}

(B.2)

By Corollary
B.2
, we find for
l<k

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{i},X_{(k-1)/n}}{d/n}\right)\right|X_{l/n}\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-c_{1}(J_{i}))^{2}}{d/n}\right)\right|X_{l/n}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-c_{2}(J_{i}))^{2}}{d/n}\right)\right|X_{l/n}\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\sqrt{\frac{d/n}{d/n+(k-l)/n}}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{(c_{1}(J_{i})-X_{l/n})^{2}}{d/n}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{(c_{2}(J_{i})-X_{l/n})^{2}}{d/n}\right)\right)\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(d)\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-l}},\end{split}

(B.3)

where it is important to note that the final constant does not depend on
J_{i}
. We now start with the first assertion of the lemma. Here, the squared terms are bounded with Lemma
B.1
and the inequalities (
B.1
), (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\int_{J_{2}}f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)^{2}\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\int_{J_{2}}\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\Big(1\wedge\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\left.\left.\left.+\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}^{c}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\lambda(J_{2})\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{2},X_{(k-1)/n})}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right)\right)\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\right]\wedge\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}\cap J_{2}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\left.\left.+\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}\cap J_{2}^{c}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\lambda(J_{2})\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{2},X_{(k-1)/n})}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right]\right)\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\right]\wedge\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\right]\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\left.\left.+\lambda(J_{2})\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{2},X_{(k-1)/n})}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right]\right)\right)\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\right).\end{split}

(B.4)

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(k-2)/n},X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-2)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{(k-1)/n})f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\right).

For the cross terms with
l<k-1
, we find by iterative conditioning, again Lemma
B.1
as well as the inequalities (
B.1
), (
B.2
) and (
B.3
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\Big[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})

\displaystyle\hskip 91.04872pt\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\mid\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\left.\cdot\int_{J_{2}}f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\Big[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\left.\left(1\wedge\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{(k-1)/n})+\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}^{c}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\lambda(J_{2})\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{2},X_{(k-1)/n})}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\Big[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mid\mathcal{F}_{l/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\left.\left.\wedge\left(\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mid\mathcal{F}_{l/n}\right]+\lambda(J_{2})\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{\nu(J_{2},X_{(k-1)/n})}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right|\mathcal{F}_{l/n}\right]\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\Big[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\right)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{l/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{l/n}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})\wedge\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{(k-l)/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})\wedge\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{l/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{1})(\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{(k-l)l}/n}\right),

where the last inequality follows as in (
B.4
). In particular, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(l-1)/n},X_{l/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(l-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{l/n})f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}}{\sqrt{(k-l)l}/n}\right)

and thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq 3C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\right)+2C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{k-2}\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}}{\sqrt{(k-l)l}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(n\wedge\left(\lambda(J_{1})\wedge\lambda(J_{2})\right)\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(n^{2}\wedge\left(\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}\right)n\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{k-2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(k-l)l}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)n\left(1\wedge\lambda(J_{1})\wedge\lambda(J_{2})\right)+C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)n^{2}\left(1\wedge\lambda(J_{1})^{2}\wedge\lambda(J_{2})^{2}\right).

This gives the first assertion of the lemma. The second one is proven similarly. Here, we have for the squared terms using Jensen’s inequality and (
B.4
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[f_{n}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1}}(X_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{J_{2}}(X_{k/n})\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(c)\left(1\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{1})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\wedge\frac{\lambda(J_{2})}{\sqrt{k/n}}\right).

Again, the cross terms for
l=k-1
give the same bound by Cauchy-Schwarz’ inequality. Up to slight modification, the case
l<k-1
works analogously as above using Lemma
B.1
and the inequalities (
B.1
), (
B.2
) and (
B.3
).
∎

Lemma B.4
.

Let
C>0
and
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence with
a_{n}\geq 0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we have

\left|\log\left(C+(1-C)\exp(-a_{n})\right)\right|\leq a_{n}\max\left\{|1-C|,\frac{|1-C|}{1\wedge C}\right\}.

By the basic inequality
x/(1+x)\leq\log(1+x)\leq x
for all
x>-1
we find

\left|\log\left(C+(1-C)\exp(-a_{n})\right)\right|\leq\max\left\{|1-C|\left|\exp(-a_{n})-1\right|,\frac{|1-C|\left|\exp(-a_{n})-1\right|}{|C+(1-C)\exp(-a_{n})|}\right\}.

Furthermore,
|C+(1-C)\exp(-a_{n})|\geq 1\wedge C
and because
1-x\leq e^{-x}
for
x\geq 0
, we have
|\exp(-a_{n})-1|\leq a_{n}
. This gives the desired result.
∎

C
Local time estimator for OBM

In Theorem
2
in
Mazzonetto (2026)
it is shown that the local time estimator
\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}
given by

\displaystyle\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}:=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho)(X_{k/n}-\rho)<0\}}

(C.1)

is a consistent estimator of the local time
L_{1}^{\rho}(X)
. From this, it follows (details are given below) that for any
K>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\rho\in U_{K/n}(\rho_{0})}\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}0.

(C.2)

Then, Proposition
1.2
reveals that the local time estimator evaluated in the MLE
\hat{\rho}_{n}
is again a consistent estimator for
L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)
, i.e.

\displaystyle\hat{L}_{n}^{\hat{\rho}_{n}}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X).

First, we estimate

\sup_{\rho\in U_{K/n}(\rho_{0})}\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right|\leq\sup_{\rho\in U_{K/n}(\rho_{0})}\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho_{0}}\right|+\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho_{0}}-L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right|,

where the second term converges to zero in probability by Theorem
2
in
Mazzonetto (2026)
. For the other one, we have by definition of
\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}
that for
\rho\leq\rho_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho_{0}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho)(X_{k/n}-\rho)<0\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})(X_{k/n}-\rho_{0})<0\}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho,\rho<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho<X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},X_{k/n}<\rho\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\left.+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho<X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},X_{k/n}>\rho_{0}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}>\rho_{0},\rho<X_{k/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\right)

Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{\rho_{0}-K/n\leq\rho\leq\rho_{0}}\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho_{0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}-K/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}-K/n<X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\right)

By Lemma
B.3
,

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}-K/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}-K/n<X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\right)\right)^{2}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(K)\frac{1}{n}

and thus,

\sup_{\rho_{0}-K/n\leq\rho\leq\rho_{0}}\left|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho_{0}}\right|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}0.

The same argument works for
\sup_{\rho_{0}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{0}+K/n}|\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}-\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho_{0}}|
and (
C.2
) follows.
∎

D
Simulation study

This section contains a simulation of the distribution of the limit
\mathrm{argsup}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\ell(z)
together with a study on coverage of the asymptotic confidence set (
1.8
). In all simulations, we used the smallest member of
\mathrm{Argsup}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}f(z)
in case of a non-unique maximizer. Moreover, we use the family

\big(\hat{L}_{n}^{\rho}\big)_{\rho\in\mathbb{R}}

given in (
C.1
) and introduced in
Mazzonetto (2026)
to estimate the local time.

In Figure
D.1
, the Lebesgue density of
\mathrm{argsup}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\ell(z)
is visualized together with the histogram density estimator of

n\hat{L}_{n}^{\hat{\rho}_{n}}(\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho).

One can observe that this distribution is not symmetric around zero. It is skewed to the left in case
\alpha<\beta
and skewed ot the right if
\alpha>\beta
. This is reasonable in view of the definition of
\ell(z)
given in (
1.6
).
Moreover, we can observe that the variance of
\mathrm{argsup}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\ell(z)
decreases the larger
|\alpha-\beta|
. This is reasonable as in this case
|b_{\alpha,\beta}|
and
|b_{\alpha,\beta}^{\prime}|
increase, corresponding to a steeper (negative) drift for the function
\ell(z)
. This confirms the heuristics that estimation of
\rho_{0}
is easier the more
\alpha
and
\beta
differ from each other.

Fig D.1:
The black solid line visualizes the Lebesgue density
f_{\mathrm{est}}
of
\mathrm{argsup}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\ell(z)
for
n=1,000
based on
M=50,000
samples. The histogram density estimator is based on
N=1,000
samples of
n\hat{L}_{n}^{\hat{\rho}_{n}}(\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho)
, where the underlying processes were generated on a grid of size
10^{-5}
. In (a), we chose
\alpha=0.5
,
\beta=0.65
and
\rho_{0}=0
. In (b) we set
\alpha=0.65
,
\beta=1
and
\rho_{0}=0
.

In Table
D.2
we give the proportion of samples lying in the
(1-\kappa)
-confidence interval (
1.8
). One can observe that the coverage is already good for small sample sizes
n
. For this simulation, all sample paths were started in the true parameter
x_{0}=\rho_{0}
to ensure that the condition
\{L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)>0\}
is satisfied with probability one (which allows to take into account every sample path, keeping the computational cost low).

E
Expansion of the normalized log-likelihood function

In this section, we give some expansions of the normalized log-likelhood expressions

\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{9}\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{I_{j,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}}

depending on the regime
I_{j,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}
. Those are used in the proof of Propositions
3.1
and
3.4
and thus contribute to both the
n
-consistency and the limiting distribution. Some expansions are given as a leading term and a remainder, for which the following observation is crucial:

•

The order of the remainders of the case
I_{j,k}
for different
j
may be different as expressions of
n
and
\theta
. Remarkably, however, their conditional expectations given
X_{(k-1)/n}
are of the same order for all
j=1,\dots,9
(as used in Propositions
3.1
and
3.4
).

•

The remainder terms are of the same order as the leading terms for
|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|\asymp n^{-1/2}
, whereas they are of smaller order for
|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|\ll n^{-1/2}
.

For the presentation, we always assume
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
, use the notation
I_{j,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}
as given in (
3.2
) and denote by
\xi_{k}
an intermediate value of the corresponding Lagrange form in the Taylor expansion. We write
\xi_{k}
to highlight its (possible) dependence on
X_{(k-1)/n}
and
X_{k/n}
. In particular,
\theta^{\prime}\leq\xi_{k}\leq\theta
and
\xi_{k}
may be different in different expressions.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{I_{1,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}}
.

Here, we have
X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}
and present both a first and second order Taylor expansion. The first order expansion is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt=-\left(\theta-\theta^{\prime}\right)\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\xi_{k}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n};1/n)}\frac{2(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})}{(\alpha^{2}/n)\sqrt{2\pi\alpha^{2}/n}}\\
&\hskip 34.14322pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\end{split}

(E.1)

and the second order expansion by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt=-\left(\theta-\theta^{\prime}\right)\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n};1/n)}\frac{2(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\theta^{\prime}+X_{(k-1)/n})}{(\alpha^{2}/n)\sqrt{2\pi\alpha^{2}/n}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\theta^{\prime}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\theta-\theta^{\prime}\right)^{2}\left[\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}/n}-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{4(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{\alpha^{4}/n^{2}}\right]\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\cdot\frac{1}{P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\xi_{k}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n};1/n)}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\alpha^{2}/n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-\left(\theta-\theta^{\prime}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\right)^{2}\frac{4(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{[P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\xi_{k}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n};1/n)]^{2}}\frac{1}{2\pi\alpha^{6}/n^{3}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt=:-\left(\theta-\theta^{\prime}\right)\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n};1/n)}\frac{2(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\theta^{\prime}+X_{(k-1)/n})}{(\alpha^{2}/n)\sqrt{2\pi\alpha^{2}/n}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\theta^{\prime}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}+R_{1}(k,\theta^{\prime},\theta).\end{split}

(E.2)

Using boundedness of
x\mapsto(1+x^{2})\exp(-x^{2}/4)
and that fact that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k/n}-2\rho_{0}-2\xi_{k}+X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)}{P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\xi_{k}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n};1/n)n^{-1/2}}\right|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=\sqrt{2\pi}\alpha\left|\frac{1}{\exp\left(\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}/n}(X_{(k/n}-\rho_{0}-\xi_{k})(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-\xi_{k})\right)-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}}\right|\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\end{split}

(E.3)

for
X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\xi_{k}
, we obtain
