. Next, we observe that the supremum within the expectation can be bounded explicitly in terms of
\theta
only:
By the decomposition (
G.19
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in[\theta-C/n,\theta]}\left|M_{n}(\theta)-M_{n}(\theta^{\prime})\right|\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in[\theta-C/n,\theta]}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(1+\frac{n}{2}(X_{k/n}-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right)\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(1+\frac{n}{2}(X_{k/n}-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\left.\left.\cdot\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right)\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\Bigg)\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(1+\frac{n}{2}(X_{k/n}-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-C/n<X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-C/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right)\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(1+\frac{n}{2}(X_{k/n}-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\left.\left.\cdot\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-C/n<X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-C/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right)\right|X_{(k-1)/n}\right].\end{split}

(G.21)

The supremum over
\theta^{\prime}\in[\theta,\theta+C/n]
can be dealt with in the same way, starting with a similar decomposition as (
G.19
). By Lemma
B.3
, the second moment of the bound in (
G.21
) is bounded uniformly in
n
and
\theta
. Thus, the claim of the this lemma is shown due to (
G.20
).
∎

G.2
Remaining proofs of Subsection
4.2

We will first specify
\kappa_{0}
and
\zeta_{1}
such that by the first inequality is valid for
|\theta|\leq\kappa_{0}/\sqrt{n}
with
\zeta=\zeta_{1}
and then show that the second one is valid for the remaining
\kappa_{0}/\sqrt{n}\leq|\theta|\leq K/\sqrt{n}
and some
\zeta_{2}
. The claim then follows for
\zeta=\min\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}
. By Proposition
3.1
we have for
|\theta|\leq\kappa/\sqrt{n}
,

B_{n}(\theta)\leq-n|\theta|\left(\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta\geq 0\}}F_{\alpha,\beta}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta<0\}}\tilde{F}_{\alpha,\beta}\right)\frac{1}{n}\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)-\frac{r_{n}(1,\theta)}{n|\theta|}\right),

where
\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)
is given in (
3.7
) and

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sup_{|\theta^{\prime}|\leq|\theta|}\frac{|r_{n}(1,\theta^{\prime})|}{|\theta^{\prime}|}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}|\theta|n^{3/2}.

By Lemma
3.2
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}+\frac{1}{\beta}\right)L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X),

(G.22)

such that for
n\geq n_{0}
and
n_{0}
large enough, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}(E_{n})>1-\epsilon/6
for

E_{n}:=\left\{\frac{1}{n}\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)\geq\frac{L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)(\alpha+\beta)}{4\alpha\beta}\right\}.

Recall
A_{2}\cap A_{3}
from (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) with
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}(\{L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)>0\}\cap A_{2}\cap A_{3})\geq 1-\epsilon/6
for suitable constants in the definition of
A_{2},A_{3}
and define

A_{n}^{\prime}(\kappa):=A_{2}\cap A_{3}\cap E_{n}\cap\left\{\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\leq\kappa/\sqrt{n}}\frac{|r_{n}(1,\theta^{\prime})|}{|\theta^{\prime}|}\leq C_{r}n\kappa\right\}.

By Markov’s inequality,

\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}\left(\sup_{|\theta^{\prime}|\leq\kappa/\sqrt{n}}\frac{|r_{n}(1,\theta^{\prime})|}{|\theta^{\prime}|}>C_{r}n\kappa\right)\leq\frac{C_{\alpha,\beta}\kappa}{C_{r}\kappa}=\frac{C_{\alpha,\beta}}{C_{r}},

and we can choose
C_{r}
independently of
n
and
\kappa
large enough such that
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}(A_{n}^{\prime}(\kappa))\geq 1-\epsilon/2
for
n\geq n_{0}
. Then we have for
|\theta|\leq\kappa/\sqrt{n}
,

B_{n}(\theta)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{\prime}(\kappa)}\leq-n|\theta|\left(\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta\geq 0\}}F_{\alpha,\beta}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta<0\}}\tilde{F}_{\alpha,\beta}\right)\frac{\xi(\alpha+\beta)}{4\alpha\beta}-C_{r}\kappa\right).

For

\kappa_{0}:=\frac{\xi(\alpha+\beta)}{8C_{r}\alpha\beta}\min\{F_{\alpha,\beta},\tilde{F}_{\alpha,\beta}\},

the first assertion of the lemma then follows with
\zeta=\zeta_{1}:=C_{r}\kappa_{0}
. Next, we consider
\kappa_{0}/\sqrt{n}\leq|\theta|\leq K/\sqrt{n}
. Denoting by
\mathbb{P}_{\rho,1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}}(\cdot)=\mathbb{P}_{\rho}(X_{k/n}\in\cdot\mid X_{(k-1)/n})
the distribution of
X_{k/n}
given
X_{(k-1)/n}
in our model with parameter
\rho
and by
KL(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{2})
the Kullback–Leibler divergence of two probability measures
\mathbb{P}_{1}
,
\mathbb{P}_{2}
, we observe

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right)\ \right|\ X_{(k-1)/n}\right]=-KL\left(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}},\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}+\theta,1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}}\right).

By the first Pinsker inequality (
Tsybakov (2009)
, Lemma
2.5
) we have

KL\left(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}},\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}+\theta,1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}}\right)\geq 2d_{\textrm{TV}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}},\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}+\theta,1/n}^{X_{(k-1)/n}}\right)^{2},

where
d_{\textrm{TV}}
denots total variation distance. By Scheffé’s theorem (
Tsybakov (2009)
, Lemma
2.1
) we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\log\left(\frac{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}{p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n})}\right)\ \right|\ X_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq-\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy\right)^{2}.

Consequently, by (
4.14
) (which is proven right after this proof),

B_{n}(\theta)\leq-\frac{1}{2}\left(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{1}\right)^{2}n\theta^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\exp\left(-\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}-2\left(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}K\right)^{2}\right).

From Lemma
3.2
, we find for
f(x):=\exp(-2x^{2}/\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}-2(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}K)^{2})
that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\exp\left(-\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}-2\left(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}K\right)^{2}\right)\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}\lambda_{\alpha,\beta}(f)L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X).

Then, for

\displaystyle A_{n}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=A_{2}\cap A_{3}\cap\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\exp\left(-\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}-2\left(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}K\right)^{2}\right)>\frac{\lambda_{\alpha,\beta}(f)}{2}L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)\right\}

we obtain
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}(A_{n}^{\prime\prime})\geq 1-\epsilon/2
for
n
large enough and

B_{n}(\theta)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{\prime\prime}}\leq-\frac{1}{2}\left(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{1}\right)^{2}\frac{\lambda_{\alpha,\beta}(f)}{2}\xi n^{3/2}\theta^{2}.

Thus, the second inequality in the statement is true for
\zeta=\zeta_{2}:=(C_{\alpha,\beta}^{1})^{2}\lambda_{\alpha,\beta}(f)\xi/4
and the statement follows for
\zeta=\min\{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\}
and the sets
A_{n}:=A_{n}^{\prime}(\kappa_{0})\cap A_{n}^{\prime\prime}
.
∎

We prove the statement for
\theta\geq 0
. The case
\theta<0
works the same. For the proof, we distinguish the cases
X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}
,
\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta
and
X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}+\theta
. Moreover, we denote
\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/2}
and by
\Phi(\cdot)
the cumulative distribution function of the standard Gaussian distribution, i.e.
\Phi(y)=\int_{-\infty}^{y}\varphi(x)dx
.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}}
.

Here, using the explicit representation of the transition density and Jensen’s inequality in the second step,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\rho_{0}+\theta}^{\infty}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\beta^{2}/n}}\left|\int_{\rho_{0}+\theta}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{n}{2}\left(\frac{y-\rho_{0}-\theta}{\beta}-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-\theta}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 156.49014pt\left.-\exp\left(-\frac{n}{2}\left(\frac{y-\rho_{0}}{\beta}-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\right)dy\right|.\end{split}

(G.23)

By substitution we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\beta^{2}/n}}\int_{\rho_{0}+\theta}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{n}{2}\left(\frac{y-\rho_{0}-\theta}{\beta}-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-\theta}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=1-\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\beta^{2}/n}}\int_{\rho_{0}+\theta}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{n}{2}\left(\frac{y-\rho_{0}}{\beta}-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=1-\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right).

The mean value theorem gives
\Phi(x+\epsilon)-\Phi(x)=\varphi(\xi_{x})\epsilon
for
x\leq\xi_{x}\leq x+\epsilon
and thus by (
G.23
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right)-\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right|\sqrt{n}\theta\varphi(\xi_{k})

for an intermediate value
\xi_{k}
satisfying

-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\max\{\alpha,\beta\}/\sqrt{n}}\leq\xi_{k}\leq-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}/\sqrt{n}}.

In particular, using
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
and the assumption
|\theta|\leq K/\sqrt{n}
, we have the final bound

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right|\theta\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\theta}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}/\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right|\theta\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{\alpha^{2}/n}-\frac{K^{2}}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}\right).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta}
.

Denoting by
\|\cdot\|_{L^{1}(\lambda)}
the
L^{1}
-norm with respect to Lebesgue measure and applying the reverse triangle inequality yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\int_{-\infty}^{\rho_{0}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\left\|\left(P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},\cdot\ ;1/n)-P_{4}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},\cdot\ ;1/n)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{0}]}(\cdot)\right\|_{L^{1}(\lambda)}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\geq\left|\left\|P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},\cdot\ ;1/n)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{0}]}(\cdot)\right\|_{L^{1}(\lambda)}-\left\|P_{4}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},\cdot\ ;1/n)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{0}]}(\cdot)\right\|_{L^{1}(\lambda)}\right|.\end{split}

(G.24)

By substitution, we find

\displaystyle\left\|P_{1}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},\cdot\ ;1/n)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{0}]}(\cdot)\right\|_{L^{1}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\Phi\left(\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)

and

\left\|P_{4}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},\cdot\ ;1/n)\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{0}]}(\cdot)\right\|_{L^{1}(\lambda)}=\frac{2\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right).

Note here that
2\beta/(\alpha+\beta)=1-(\alpha-\beta)/(\alpha+\beta)
. Using the mean value theorem for
\Phi
, we observe

\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)=\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right)-\sqrt{n}(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\varphi(\xi_{1,k})

and

\Phi\left(\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)=\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-\theta)}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\varphi(\xi_{2,k}),

for intermediate values
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
with

\min\left\{\frac{\rho_{0}-X_{(k-1)/n}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}},\frac{\rho_{0}-X_{(k-1)/n}}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right\}\leq\xi_{1,k}\leq\max\left\{\frac{\rho_{0}-X_{(k-1)/n}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}},\frac{\rho_{0}-X_{(k-1)/n}}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right\}

and

\displaystyle\min\left\{-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}},\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right\}\leq\xi_{2,k}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\leq\max\left\{-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}},\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right\}.

As
\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta
,
|X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}|\leq|X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta|
and consequently for
i=1,2
,

|\xi_{i,k}|\leq\frac{|X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta|}{2\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}.

In consequence, starting from (
G.24
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\left|\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)-\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\left.+\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\Phi\left(-\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}}{\beta/\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\Phi\left(\frac{X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\left|-\sqrt{n}(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\right)\varphi(\xi_{1})-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-\theta)}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\varphi(\xi_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha\geq\beta\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha<\beta\}}\right)\left[-\frac{2\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\sqrt{n}(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})\varphi(\xi_{1})\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\left.-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta)}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\varphi(\xi_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\geq\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha\geq\beta\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha<\beta\}}\right)\left[-\frac{2\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\sqrt{n}(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{2(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta)}{\alpha/\sqrt{n}}\right]\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta)^{2}}{2\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha\geq\beta\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha<\beta\}}\right)\frac{2(\alpha-\beta)}{(\alpha+\beta)\alpha}\sqrt{n}\left(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta)^{2}}{2\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\frac{4|1-\beta/\alpha|}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{2\pi\/n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-2\theta)^{2}}{2\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right),

where the third step uses that both summands within the absolute value have the same sign for
\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta
. Consequently, we have proven that (using again
\theta\leq K/\sqrt{n}
and
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\frac{4|1-\beta/\alpha|}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\theta\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}-\frac{2K^{2}}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}\right).

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}+\theta}
.

This case works analogously to the first one with
X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}
and is therefore omitted. It yields

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)-p_{1/n}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y)\right|dy

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right|\theta\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{\beta^{2}/n}-K^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\right)^{2}\right).

The inequality in (
4.14
) now follows from these three steps by setting

C_{\alpha,\beta}^{1}:=\min\left\{\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right|,\frac{4|1-\beta/\alpha|}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha+\beta}\left|\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right|\right\}

and

C_{\alpha,\beta}^{2}:=K^{2}\cdot\max\left\{\frac{2}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}},\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)^{2}\right\}.

∎

Note that
M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
, where
d_{k}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
are martingale increments, i.e.
\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[d_{k}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\mid\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]=0
and consequently (
G.11
) holds true. Here, for
0\leq\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
,
d_{k}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})
is given as

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left.-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left.-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt-\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left.-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right).

From this expression, we find the upper bound

\displaystyle d_{k}(\theta,\theta^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{6}\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\left.+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]^{2}\right].

With Lemma
B.1
we then find the moment bounds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\int_{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k/n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x_{0})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}k/n}\right)dy

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k/n}}|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|

and by Jensen’s inequality for conditional expectations,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k/n}}|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|.

The others work the same way and yield the same upper bound. Consequently, we have from (
G.11
) that

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k/n}}\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}n|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|.

∎

We use that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta^{\prime}\in U_{1/(C_{1}n)}(\theta)}\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\sup_{\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta}\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2},\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.\sup_{\theta\leq\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta+1/(C_{1}n)}\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right\}

and only construct an upper bound for
\sup_{\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta}\left(M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}
. The other one can be built analogously and the upper bound of the statement is then obtained as the maximum of the two upper bounds. For
\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
, we find

\displaystyle\left|M_{n}^{1}(\theta)-M_{n}^{1}(\theta^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\left.-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+\theta\}}|X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\Big)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+\theta-1/(C_{1}n)\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta\}}\mid X_{(k-1)/n}\right]\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt:=\overline{M}_{n}^{1}(\theta).

Using
(a_{1}+\dots+a_{6})^{2}\leq 2^{6}(a_{1}^{2}+\dots+a_{6}^{2})
, Lemma
B.3
reveals the second moment of
\overline{M}_{n}^{1}(\theta)
is bounded with a bound independent of
\theta
and hence (
4.21
) follows.
∎

H
Stable convergence of piecewise constant processes towards discontinuous conditional PIIs

In this section, we present a modification of the stable limit results given in Theorem
4.1
in
Jacod (2003)
and Theorem
2.1
in
Jacod (1997)
, tailored to our context. The result in
Jacod (1997)
is to the best of our knowledge the only one that is applicable for infill asymptotics without a certain nestedness condition on the filtration, but only covers a continuous (in time) limit and thus can not be applied to the process
\ell_{n,t}
given in (
1.9
) as the Lindeberg-type condition
(2.12)
in Theorem
2.1
in this paper is not satisfied (see Section
5
). On the other hand, Theorem
4.1
in
Jacod (2003)
covers limit processes with jumps but does not allow in its current formulation to treat convergence of processes
X^{n}
, where each
X^{n}
is defined on a different stochastic basis
\mathcal{B}^{n}
.

Starting from a filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\mathbb{P})
, we work on a very good extension of this space in the sense of Section
2
in
Jacod (2003)
. We prove a result on stable convergence of a piecewise constant process towards an
\mathcal{F}
-conditional process with independent increments (PII) which is allowed to be discontinuous. For more details on the notion of
\mathcal{F}
-conditional PIIs, the reader is referred to
Jacod (2003)
. Throughout this section, we assume the following:

•

(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
is a probability space supporting a standard Brownian motion
W
with
\mathbb{F}=(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
being the augmented filtration induced by
W
and restrict attention to the case
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{1}
.
Then, the stochastic basis
\mathcal{B}:=(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{F},\mathbb{P})
has the martingale representation property with respect to
W
(see Theorem
19.11
in
Kallenberg (2021)
).

•

Corresponding to
\mathbb{F}
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we introduce the discretized filtration
\mathbb{F}^{n}=(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{n})_{t\in[0,1]}
via
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{n}:=\mathcal{F}_{\lfloor nt\rfloor/n}
.

•

We define the process
W^{n}=(W_{t}^{n})_{t\in[0,1]}
via

W_{t}^{n}:=W_{\lfloor nt\rfloor}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}.

Then
W^{n}
is a square-integrable
\mathbb{F}^{n}
-martingale and we have

\langle W^{n}\rangle_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\langle W\rangle_{t}=t\quad\textrm{ for all }t\in[0,1].

•

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
X^{n}
be a
\mathbb{F}^{n}
-semimartingale with

X_{t}^{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\chi_{nk},

where
\chi_{nk}
is
\mathcal{F}_{k/n}^{n}
-measurable and square-integrable.

•

We now consider the
\mathcal{F}^{n}
-semimartingale
(X^{n},W^{n})
. Its first characteristic
B^{n}
, its second modified characteristic
C^{n}
and its third characteristic
\nu^{n}
are given as (see Theorem II.3.11(b) and II.3.18 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
)

B^{n}=\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}\mid\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]\\
0\end{pmatrix},\qquad C^{n}=\begin{pmatrix}\langle X^{n},X^{n}\rangle&\langle X^{n},W^{n}\rangle\\
\langle X^{n},W^{n}\rangle&\langle W^{n},W^{n}\rangle\end{pmatrix},

and for any measurable
g:\mathbb{R}^{2}\longrightarrow[0,\infty)

\left(g\star\nu^{n}\right)_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.g\left(\chi_{nk},W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right)\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

Proposition H.1
.

Assume there exists a continuous process
B
with finite variation and a random measure
\nu
on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
not charging
[0,1]\times\{0\}
and satisfying
\nu(\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=0
identically, such that the following convergences hold for all
t\in[0,1]
:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq t}|B_{s}^{n}-B_{s}|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0,

(H.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{nk}-\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}(f\star\nu)_{t}\quad\textrm{ for }f(x,y):=x^{2},

(H.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{nk}-\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0,

(H.3)

\displaystyle(g\star\nu^{n})_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}(g\star\nu)_{t}\quad\textrm{ for all }g\in\mathcal{C},

(H.4)

where
\mathcal{C}
is a countable set of Lipschitz-continuous bounded nonnegative functions on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, vanishing in a neighbourhood of
0
and being a measure-determining class for measures not charging
0
. Moreover, we assume there exists a constant
\kappa>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\chi_{nk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}\right]\longrightarrow\ 0.

(H.5)

Then there exist a very good extension
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
of
\mathcal{B}
and a quasi-left continuous process
X
on
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
which is an
\mathcal{F}
-conditional PII and the pair
(X,W)
admits the characteristics
(B,C,\nu)
, where
C=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathrm{id}_{[0,1]}\end{pmatrix}
.
Moreover,
X^{n}
converges stably in law to
X
.

We define processes
\tilde{W}^{n}=(\tilde{W}^{n}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
and
\tilde{X}^{n}=(\tilde{X}^{n}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
via

\tilde{W}^{n}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{\{|W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}|\leq 1\}}

and (recall the constant
\kappa
from (
H.5
))

\tilde{X}^{n}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\chi_{nk}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|\leq\kappa\}}.

For the latter we have using (
H.5
),

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq 1}|X_{t}^{n}-\tilde{X}_{t}^{n}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\chi_{nk}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\chi_{nk}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\ 0.

Assuming we have shown
\mathcal{F}
-stable convergence
\tilde{X}^{n}\longrightarrow X
, then the claim of
\mathcal{F}
-stable convergence
X^{n}\longrightarrow X
follows by Theorem
3.18
(a) in
Häusler and
Luschgy (2015)
together with the previously shown uniform stochastic convergence of
\tilde{X}^{n}
towards
X^{n}
. Thus, in what follows we establish

\displaystyle\tilde{X}^{n}\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}-st}}{{\longrightarrow}}\ X.

(H.6)

This proof is conducted in eight steps.

(i)

Denote by
\tilde{B}^{n}
and
\tilde{\nu}^{n}
the first and third characteristic of
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})
. We now show that (
H.1
)-(
H.4
) hold true with the same limits for
\tilde{X}^{n}
in place of
X^{n}
and
\tilde{W}^{n}
replacing
W^{n}
, i.e. we prove for all
t\in[0,1]
,
g\in\mathcal{C}
and
f(x,y)=x^{2}
that:

(a)

\sup_{s\leq t}|\tilde{B}_{s}^{n}-B_{s}|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
,

(b)

\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}\rangle_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}(f\star\nu)_{t}
,

(c)

\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W^{n}}\rangle_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
,

(d)

(g\star\tilde{\nu}^{n})_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}(g\star\nu)_{t}
,

and additionally

(e)

\langle\tilde{W}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\ t
.

We start with claim (a). Here, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq t}|\tilde{B}_{s}^{n}-B^{n}_{s}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\chi_{nk}|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\kappa}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\chi_{nk}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

The last expression converges to zero in probability by (
H.5
) and thus (a) follows by (
H.1
). For part (b), we note that

\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}\rangle_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{nk}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|\leq\kappa\}}-\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|\leq\kappa\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

and

\langle X^{n},X^{n}\rangle_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{nk}-\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for symmetric positive semidefinite bilinear forms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}\rangle_{t}-\langle X^{n},X^{n}\rangle_{t}|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\langle\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n}\rangle_{t}+2|\langle X^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n}\rangle_{t}|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\langle\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n}\rangle_{t}+\sqrt{|\langle X^{n},X^{n}\rangle_{t}||\langle\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n}\rangle_{t}|}.\end{split}

(H.7)

Now,

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n}\rangle_{t}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\chi_{nk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0

(H.8)

by (
H.5
). Then, (b) follows from (
H.7
) and
\langle X^{n},X^{n}\rangle_{t}\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}(f\star\nu)_{t}
given in (
H.2
). The reasoning for (c) is similar to that of (b). Here, we first observe that with the abbreviation
\tilde{w}_{nk}=(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{\{|W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}|\leq 1\}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\chi_{nk}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|\leq\kappa\}}-\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|\leq\kappa\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\cdot(\tilde{w}_{nk}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{w}_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])\big|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\Big]

and

\langle X^{n},W^{n}\rangle_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{nk}-\mathbb{E}[\chi_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

Then, again by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for symmetric and positive semidefinite bilinear forms,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle_{t}-\langle X^{n},W^{n}\rangle_{t}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\langle\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle_{t}+\langle X^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n}\rangle_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\langle\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}-X^{n}\rangle_{t}\langle\tilde{W}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle_{t}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sqrt{\langle\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n}\rangle_{t}\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}\rangle_{t}}.

The first summand converges to zero in probability by (
H.8
), for the second one it follows by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n}\rangle_{t}\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}|>1\}}\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})^{4}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}|>1)}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{\lfloor nt\rfloor}{n}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}\left(|Z|>\sqrt{n}\right)}\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

(H.9)

where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
denotes a standard Gaussian random variable. Then, condition (c) follows by (
H.3
). To prove (d), we abbreviate
\tilde{w}_{nk}=(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})\mathbbm{1}_{\{|W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}|\leq 1\}}
and bound

\displaystyle\left|(g\star\tilde{\nu}^{n})_{t}-(g\star\nu^{n})_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.|g(\chi_{nk}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|\leq\kappa\}},\tilde{w}_{nk})-g(\chi_{nk},W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n})|\right|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 2\|g\|_{\sup}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{|W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}|>1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 2\|g\|_{\sup}\left(\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}\left[\chi_{nk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\chi_{nk}|>\kappa\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]+\lfloor nt\rfloor\mathbb{P}\left(|Z|>\sqrt{n}\right)\right),

where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
is a standard Gaussian random variable. The last expression now tends to zero by (
H.5
) and the Gaussian tail inequality. Then by (
H.4
), (d) follows. Following the steps for (b), it is sufficient for (e) to show that

\langle\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}-W^{n}\rangle_{t}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\ 0,

which is a direct consequence of (
H.9
).

(ii)

We denote the second modified characteristic of
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})
by
\tilde{G}^{n}
, i.e.

\tilde{G}^{n}:=\begin{pmatrix}\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}\rangle&\langle\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle\\
\langle\tilde{W}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}\rangle&\langle\tilde{W}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle\end{pmatrix}

and define
G=(G_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
via

G_{t}=\begin{pmatrix}(f\star\nu)_{t}&0\\
0&t\end{pmatrix}.

For any unit vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, by Proposition II.2.17(b) in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
,
u^{T}\tilde{G}_{t}^{n}u
is non-decreasing in
t
; the same is obviously true for
u^{T}G_{t}u
. By a standard argument (that allows to deduce uniform convergence from pointwise convergence of non-decreasing functions to a non-decreasing limiting function), we then obtain

\sup_{t\leq 1}\left|u^{T}\tilde{G}_{t}^{n}u-u^{T}G_{t}u\right|=\sup_{t\leq 1}\left|u^{T}(\tilde{G}_{t}^{n}-G_{t})u\right|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0.

By setting
u
to the standard basis vectors, we conclude uniform stochastic convergence of the diagonal terms. Subsequently choosing
u=(1/\sqrt{2},1/\sqrt{2})
gives uniform stochastic convergence of the off-diagonal entries and we conclude for any matrix norm
\|\cdot\|
,

\sup_{s\leq t}\|\tilde{G}_{s}^{n}-G_{s}\|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0\qquad\textrm{ for all }t\in[0,1].

(iii)

By the convergences (
H.1
)-(
H.4
) and that of
\langle\tilde{W}^{n}\rangle
, the processes
B
,
G
and
g\star\nu
for
g\in\mathcal{C}
are
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-adapted. By assumption,
B
is continuous in
t
, and for
G
and
g\star\nu
, continuity in
t
follows from the assumption
\nu(\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=0
. Therefore,
B,C
and
g\star\nu
are adapted and continuous processes, hence
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-predictable.

(iv)

From
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-predictability of
B,G
and
\nu
(which was established in (iii)), we can deduce from Theorem
3.2
in
Jacod (2003)
that there exists a very good extension
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{B}
and a quasi-left continuous process
X
on
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
which is an
\mathcal{F}
-conditional PII and the pair
(X,W)
admits the characteristics
(B,C,\nu)
. By the same result,
X
can be realized as follows: Let
(\hat{\Omega},\hat{\mathcal{F}},(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]})
be the canonical space of all càdlàg functions and
X
the canonical process. Then
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}(d\omega,d\hat{\omega})=\mathbb{P}(d\omega)Q_{\omega}(d\hat{\omega})
and
Q_{\omega}
is entirely determined by the first coordinate of
B
,
(f\star\nu)
and
\nu(dt,dx,\{0\})
.

(v)

As the
\sigma
-field generated by
W
is countably generated, by Proposition
3.4.5
in
Cohn (2013)
there exists a countable collection
\{V_{m}:m\in\mathbb{N}\}
of bounded random variables which is dense in
L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. We set
N^{m}:=(N_{t}^{m})_{t\in[0,1]}
with
N_{t}^{m}=\mathbb{E}[V_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]
. According to IX.7.9 and IX.7.10 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
, we have

(A)

Every bounded martingale on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\mathbb{P})
is the limit in
L^{2}
, locally uniformly in time, of a sequence of sums of stochastic integrals w.r.t. a finite number of different
N^{m}
.

(B)

(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
is the smallest filtration, up to
\mathbb{P}
-null sets, w.r.t. which all
N^{m}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, are adapted.

(vi)

For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
N(n)^{m}=(N(n)_{t}^{m})_{t\in[0,1]}
via
N(n)_{t}^{m}=N_{\lfloor nt\rfloor/n}^{m}
. As
N^{m}
is bounded, we clearly have
\sup_{\omega,t,n}|N(n)_{t}^{m}(\omega)|<\infty
and because
N^{m}
is continuous by the martingale representation property, we find

\left(\tilde{W}^{n},N(n)^{1},\dots,N(n)^{m}\right)\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\ \left(W,N^{1},\dots,N^{m}\right)

in the Skorohod space
\mathcal{D}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{m+1})
.

We can consider
\mathcal{N}=(N^{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathcal{N}(n)=(N^{m}(n))_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
as a process with paths in the Skorohod space
\mathcal{D}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{n}=(g\star\tilde{\nu}^{n})_{g\in\mathcal{C}}
,
\mathcal{H}=(g\star\nu)_{g\in\mathcal{C}}
as processes in
\mathcal{D}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{C}})
. By our convergence assumption and (ii), we have

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{B}^{n},\tilde{G}^{n},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n},\mathcal{N}(n)\right)\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\ \left(B,G,\mathcal{H},W,\mathcal{N}\right)

(H.10)

in the Skorohod sense. As the jumps of
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})
are uniformly bounded, Theorem VI.4.18 and Lemma VI.4.22 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
together with (ii) reveal that
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})
is tight in
\mathcal{D}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{2})
. The process on the right-hand side of (
H.10
) is continuous and we conclude with Corollary VI.3.33 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
that
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n},\tilde{B}^{n},\tilde{G}^{n},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n},\mathcal{N}(n))
is tight in the respective Skorohod space. Moreover, for any limiting process
(\hat{X},\hat{W},\hat{B},\hat{G},\hat{\mathcal{H}},\hat{\mathcal{N}})
, we have
\mathcal{L}(\hat{W},\hat{B},\hat{G},\hat{\mathcal{H}},\hat{\mathcal{N}})=\mathcal{L}(W,G,B,\mathcal{H},\mathcal{N})
.

(vii)

We now choose any subsequence, indexed in
n_{k}
such that the sequence of distributions
\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}^{n_{k}},\tilde{W}^{n_{k}},\tilde{B}^{n_{k}},\tilde{G}^{n_{k}},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{n_{k}},\mathcal{N}(n_{k}))
converges weakly to some measure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
on the corresponding image. From what precedes, one can realize the limit as follows: Consider again the canonical space
(\hat{\Omega},\hat{\mathcal{F}},(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]})
of real-valued càdlàg functions on
[0,1]
with the canonical process
X
. Then we set
\tilde{\Omega}=\Omega\times\hat{\Omega}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}=\mathcal{F}\otimes\hat{\mathcal{F}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}=\bigcap_{s>t}\mathcal{F}_{s}\otimes\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
. Since
\mathcal{F}=\sigma(V_{m}:m\in\mathbb{N})
up to
\mathbb{P}
-null sets, the pullback measure of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
is a measure on
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}})
, in particular there exists a probability measure
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
on
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}})
whose
\Omega
-marginal is
\mathbb{P}
, and such that
\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}^{n_{k}},\tilde{W}^{n_{k}},\tilde{B}^{n_{k}},\tilde{G}^{n_{k}},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{n_{k}},\mathcal{N}(n_{k}))
converges weakly to the law of
(X,W,B,G,\mathcal{H},\mathcal{N})
under
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
.

Therefore, we have an extension
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}=(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
of
\mathcal{B}=(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]},\mathbb{P})
with a disintegration
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}(d\omega,d\hat{\omega})=\mathbb{P}(d\omega)\tilde{Q}_{\omega}(d\hat{\omega})
(since
(\hat{\Omega},\hat{\mathcal{F}})
is Polish, see II.1.2 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
). Up to
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-null sets, the filtrations
(\mathcal{F})_{t\in[0,1]}
and
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
are generated by
\mathcal{N}
and
(\mathcal{N},X)
, respectively (this follows from property (B)).

We now show that
(X,W)
is a semimartingale on the stochastic basis
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
with respective characteristics
(B,C,\nu)
. To this aim, denote by
\mu^{n}
the jump measure of
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})
. Then all components of
\mathcal{N}(n)
,
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})-\tilde{B}^{n}
and
(g\star\mu^{n}-g\star\tilde{\nu}^{n})_{g\in\mathcal{C}}

are
\mathbb{F}^{n}
-local martingales with uniformly bounded jumps. Additionally, by weak convergence of
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})
to
(X,W)
, Corollary VI.2.8 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
provides weak convergence of
g\star\mu^{n}
to
g\star\mu
for all
g\in\mathcal{C}
. Hence, Proposition IX.1.17 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
yields that all components of (
\mu
denoting the jump measure associated to
(X,W)
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N},\quad(X,W)\quad\textrm{ and }\quad(g\star\mu-g\star\nu)_{g\in\mathcal{C}}

(H.11)

are
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-local martingales. Let
\tilde{L}^{n}=(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n})-\tilde{B}^{n}
and
L=(X,W)-B
, which should be read as a column vector. Identify
(\tilde{L}^{n},\tilde{G}^{n}),(L,G)
as elements of
\mathbb{R}^{6}
and introduce for the Euclidean norm
\|\cdot\|_{2}
the stopping times

\displaystyle\tau_{R}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\inf\{t\in[0,1]:\|(\tilde{L}_{t}^{n},\tilde{G}_{t}^{n})\|_{2}\geq R\textrm{ or }\|(\tilde{L}_{t-}^{n},\tilde{G}_{t-}^{n})\|_{2}\geq R\}\wedge 1,

\displaystyle\tau_{R}

\displaystyle:=\inf\{t\in[0,1]:\|(L_{t},G_{t})\|_{2}\geq R\textrm{ or }\|(L_{t-},G_{t-})\|_{2}\geq R\}\wedge 1.

Following the construction in the proof of Proposition IX.1.17 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
, it is possible to find a sequence
(R_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
with
R_{m}\nearrow\infty
such that the weak convergence in (v) can be extended to show
\mathcal{L}((\tilde{L}^{n})^{\tau_{R_{m}}^{n}},(\tilde{G}^{n})^{\tau_{R_{m}}^{n}},\tilde{X}^{n},\mathcal{N}(n))
converges weakly (in
\mathcal{D}([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}})
) to the law of
(L^{\tau_{R_{m}}},G^{\tau_{R_{m}}},X,\mathcal{N})
. Then the
\mathbb{F}^{n}
-local martingale
(\tilde{L}^{n})^{\tau_{R_{m}}^{n}}((\tilde{L}^{n})^{\tau_{R_{m}}^{n}})^{T}-(\tilde{G}^{n})^{\tau_{R_{m}}^{n}}
has bounded jumps and Proposition IX.1.17 in
Jacod and Shiryaev (2003)
proves the bounded limit
L^{\tau_{R_{m}}}(L^{\tau_{R_{m}}})^{T}-G^{\tau_{R_{m}}}
to be a
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-martingale. As
L
and
G
are càdlàg,
\tau_{R_{m}}\nearrow 1
for
m\to\infty
which implies that
LL^{T}-G
is a
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
-local martingale. This was also shown to be true for the processes in (
H.11
), and we conclude that
(X,W)
is a semimartingale on
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
with characteristics
(B,C,\nu)
. Because all elements of
\mathcal{N}
are
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
-martingales, property (A) of step (v) gives that every martingale on
\mathcal{B}
is also a martingale on
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
and our extension is very good. Then, Theorem
3.2
of
Jacod (2003)
states that the conditional
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-law of
X
knowing
\mathcal{F}
is entirely determined by
W
and the characteristics of the pair
(X,W)
. In particular, compare (iv), we have
\tilde{Q}_{\omega}=Q_{\omega}
for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega\in\Omega
and the original sequence
(\tilde{X}^{n},\tilde{W}^{n},\tilde{B}^{n},\tilde{G}^{n},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n},\mathcal{N}(n))
converges in distribution to
(X,W,B,G,\mathcal{H},\mathcal{N})
as defined on the basis
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
.

(viii)

It remains to prove that the convergence is indeed
\mathcal{F}
-stable. This follows as in the fourth step of the proof of Theorem
2.1
in
Jacod (1997)
, but is given here for the readers convenience. From (vii) we know that the sequence
(\tilde{X}^{n},\mathcal{N}(n))
converges in law to
(X,\mathcal{N})
. In particular, if
f:\mathcal{D}([0,1],\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a bounded continuous function we find (denoting with
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}
the expectation with respect to
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
),

\mathbb{E}\left[f(\tilde{X}^{n})N(n)_{1}^{m}\right]\longrightarrow{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(X)N_{1}^{m}\right],

since
N(n)^{m}
is a component of
\mathcal{N}(n)
that is uniformly bounded in
n
. By boundedness of
N(n)^{m}
and convergence of
N(n)^{m}
to
N^{m}
in the Skorohod space in probability, we can deduce that
N(n)_{1}^{m}\rightarrow N_{1}^{m}
in
L^{1}
, hence

\mathbb{E}\left[f(\tilde{X}^{n})N_{1}^{m}\right]\longrightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(X)N_{1}^{m}\right].

Since
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[UN_{1}^{m}]=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[UV_{m}]
for any bounded
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
-measurable variable
U
, we deduce

\mathbb{E}\left[f(\tilde{X}^{n})V_{m}\right]\longrightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(X)V_{m}\right].

Finally, any bounded
\mathcal{F}
-measurable random variable
V
is the
L^{2}
-limit of a sequence in
\{V_{m}:m\in\mathbb{N}\}
(see (v)), and we find the desired stable convergence

\mathbb{E}\left[f(\tilde{X}^{n})V\right]\longrightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(X)V\right].

∎

I
Remaining proofs of Section
5

We recall the assumption that
N=m+M
and
z_{m}<\dots<z_{1}<0<z_{m+1}<\dots<z_{N}
, together with the definition

z_{0}=0,\qquad z_{j}^{-}:=z_{j},\ j=1,\dots,m\quad\textrm{ and }z_{j}^{+}:=z_{m+j},\ j=1,\dots,M.

In particular, we have

\sum_{l=1}^{N}\kappa_{l}\ell_{n,t}(z_{l})=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\ell_{n,t}(z_{l}^{-})+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\ell_{n,t}(z_{l}^{+}).

Recalling the definition of
Z_{k}^{j}(\theta^{\prime},\theta)
for
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
given in (
3.9
) and (
3.10
), we can further write

\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\ell_{n,t}(z_{l}^{-})=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\left(-\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)+\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\right)\right),

as well as

\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\ell_{n,t}(z_{l}^{+})=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)\right)\right).

We use that for the conditional variance we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[(y_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]^{2}.

Next, we have by Lemma
B.1
for
z\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z/n}}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+z/n}\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\frac{z}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right).

Analogously, one obtains

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z/n}}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}+z/n\}}\frac{z}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-z/n)^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)

and for
z<0
the estimates

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z/n,0}}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z/n\}}\frac{|z|}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0}-z/n)^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)

and

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z/n,0}}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\frac{|z|}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right).

In particular, by Corollary
B.2
, we find for
z\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]^{2}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{|z|^{2}}{n\sqrt{k}}\quad\textrm{ for }I_{k}\in\left\{I_{2,k}^{0,z/n},I_{8,k}^{0,z/n},I_{2,k}^{-z/n,0},I_{8,k}^{-z/n,0}\right\}

(I.1)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k}}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}|z|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nk}}\quad\textrm{ for }I_{k}\in\left\{I_{2,k}^{0,z/n},I_{8,k}^{0,z/n},I_{2,k}^{-z/n,0},I_{8,k}^{-z/n,0}\right\}.

(I.2)

By Proposition
3.4
, for
z\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[|Z_{k}^{j}(-z/n,0)|\big|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{|z|^{2}}{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z/n)|\big|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{|z|^{2}}{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},

such that together with (
I.1
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{1}^{-}|,|z_{1}^{+}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\longrightarrow 0.

Hence,

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[(y_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}])^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]+o_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}(1)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{l}^{-}\left(-Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)+\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt+\left.\left.\left.\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\kappa_{l}^{+}\left(Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)\right)\right)^{2}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\sum_{j,l}\sum_{Z,Z^{\prime}\in\{-Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0),Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)\}}\kappa(Z)\kappa(Z^{\prime})\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[ZZ^{\prime}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{j,l}\sum_{Z\in\{-Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0),Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)\}}\sum_{I_{k}\in\{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0},I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0},I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n},I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}\}}\hskip-28.45274pt\kappa(Z)\kappa(I_{k})\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[Z\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n},0}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+\left.\left.\left.\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)\right)^{2}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=:S_{1}(k)+S_{2}(k)+S_{3}(k).

In what follows, we will work with these summands seperately. For the first two, we bound there
L^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}})
-norm as both will turn out to be negligable. The third one gives the main contributing term and is evaluated more explicitly.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{S_{1}(k)}.

Using
|ab|\leq(a^{2}+b^{2})/2
, we obtain by Proposition
3.4
,

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[ZZ^{\prime}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right]\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[Z^{2}+(Z^{\prime})^{2}\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

In particular,

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|S_{1}(k)|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\max\{|\kappa_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{S_{2}(k)}.

By Proposition
3.4
, Cauchy-Schwarz’ inequality and (
I.2
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[Z\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[Z^{2}]\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[\mathbbm{1}_{I_{k}}]}\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\frac{1}{n^{3/4}\sqrt{k}}.

In particular,

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|S_{2}(k)|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\max\{|\kappa_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}\frac{1}{n^{3/4}\sqrt{k}}.

\bullet\ \boldsymbol{S_{3}(k)}.

First, we observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l^{\prime}}^{-}/n,0}}=0,\quad\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l^{\prime}}^{+}/n}}=0,\quad\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n},0}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l^{\prime}}^{+}/n}}=0,\\
&\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l^{\prime}}^{+}/n}}=0,\quad\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l^{\prime}}^{+}/n}}=0,\quad\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l^{\prime}}^{+}/n}}=0.\end{split}

(I.3)

Hence,

\displaystyle S_{3}(k)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)^{2}+\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)^{2}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)^{2}+\left(\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)^{2}\right|\ \mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right].

Both conditional expectations can be further split into two summands, yielding in total
S_{3}(k)=S_{31}(k)+S_{32}(k)+S_{33}(k)+S_{34}(k)
. We will now evaluate those terms seperately:

\boldsymbol{-\ S_{31}(k)}.

Here, we first observe

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left.-\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=l}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{j}^{-}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt-2\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\cdot\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=l}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{j}^{-}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=l}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{j}^{-}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\right)^{2}.

Next, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi/n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\frac{\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|}{n}+r_{n,k,l}^{31}+s_{n,k,l}^{31},

where

\displaystyle r_{n,k,l}^{31}

\displaystyle=\int_{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n}^{\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n}\left(P_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y;1/n)-P_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},\rho_{0};1/n)\right)dy,

\displaystyle s_{n,k,l}^{31}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi/n}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\frac{\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|}{n},

and by arguments analogous to those used in the evaluation of
r_{n}^{8,2}
in the proof of Proposition
3.1
, we derive with a Taylor expansion of
P_{1}^{\rho_{0}}
that

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{n,k,l}^{31}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(z_{l-1}^{-},z_{l}^{-})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}

and following the reasoning for
r_{n}^{7,2}
in the proof of Proposition
3.1
gives

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|s_{n,k,l}^{31}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(z_{l-1},z_{l})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

Next, with Lemma
B.1
we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[C_{\alpha,\beta}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n}^{\rho_{0}}\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)/n})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{|z_{m}^{-}|}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n}^{\rho_{0}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(k-1)/n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x_{0})^{2}}{2(k-1)\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{|z_{m}^{-}|^{2}}{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}.\end{split}

(I.4)

With this bound (
I.4
), we then get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[2\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.\cdot\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=l}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{j}^{-}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq 2m\left(|\kappa_{1}^{-}|+\dots+|\kappa_{m}^{-}|\right)\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=l}^{m}|\kappa_{j}^{-}|\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}m^{3}\left(|\kappa_{1}^{-}|+\dots+|\kappa_{m}^{-}|\right)^{2}|z_{m}^{-}|^{2}\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

Quite similar, (
I.4
) provides us with the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=l}^{m}\kappa_{j}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{j}^{-}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0},\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C(m)\max\{|\kappa_{1}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{m}^{-}|\}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{m}^{-}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n},X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m)\max\{|\kappa_{1}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{m}^{-}|\}^{2}|z_{m}^{-}|^{2}\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

Summing up, we have shown that

\displaystyle S_{31}(k)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|+r_{nk}^{31}+s_{nk}^{31},

where
r_{nk}^{31}+s_{nk}^{31}
is a remainder with the
L^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}})
-bound

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{nk}^{31}+s_{nk}^{31}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,z_{1}^{-},\dots,z_{m}^{-})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

\boldsymbol{-\ S_{32}(k)}.

First, we note that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{-}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{-}/n\leq\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi/n}}\frac{\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|}{n}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\beta^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}+r_{n,k,l}^{32},

where

r_{n,k,l}^{32}=\int_{z_{l}^{-}/n}^{z_{l-1}^{-}/n}\left(P_{4}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},y;1/n)-P_{4}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)/n},\rho_{0};1/n)\right)dy

and analogously to the treatment of
r_{n}^{2,2}
in the proof of Proposition
3.1
we see that

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{n,k,l}^{32}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(z_{l-1}^{-},z_{l}^{-})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

In particular,

\displaystyle S_{32}(k)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\beta^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|+r_{nk}^{32},

where
r_{nk}^{32}
is a remainder with the
L^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}})
-bound

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{nk}^{32}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,z_{1}^{-},\dots,z_{m}^{-})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

\boldsymbol{-\ S_{33}(k)}.

Analogously to
S_{32}(k)
we find

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)^{2}=\sum_{l=1}^{M}\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l-1}^{+}/n<X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{l}^{+}/n\}}

and based on that

\displaystyle S_{33}(k)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{M}\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}\right|+r_{nk}^{33}

with a remainder
r_{nk}^{33}
for which we have

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{nk}^{33}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(M,z_{1}^{+},\dots,z_{M}^{+})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

\boldsymbol{-\ S_{34}(k)}.

By the same arguments employed for
S_{31}(k)
, one obtains

\displaystyle S_{34}(k)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\beta^{2}/n}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{M}\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}\right|+r_{nk}^{34},

where
r_{nk}^{34}
satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{nk}^{34}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(M,z_{1}^{+},\dots,z_{M}^{+})\frac{1}{n\sqrt{k}}.

Summing up the results for
S_{1}(k),S_{2}(k)
and
S_{3}(k)
gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\alpha^{2}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\beta^{2})/n}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\left(\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}\right|\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}\}}\alpha^{2}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\beta^{2})/n}\right)+r_{nk},\end{split}

(I.5)

where (remember that in this proof
C_{\alpha,\beta}
may depend on
m,M,z_{1}^{-},\dots,z_{m}^{-},z_{1}^{+},\dots,z_{M}^{+}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|r_{nk}|\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M,\kappa_{m}^{-},\dots,\kappa_{M}^{+},z_{m}^{-},\dots,z_{M}^{+})\frac{1}{n^{3/4}\sqrt{k}}.

(I.6)

Summing this over
k
then give

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}r_{nk}+\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Lambda_{\alpha,\beta}^{n}\left((X_{(k-1)/n})_{1\leq k\leq\lfloor nt\rfloor}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\cdot\left(\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{l}^{-}+\dots+\kappa_{m}^{-}\right)\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{-}-z_{l-1}^{-}\right|\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left.\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\left(\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\kappa_{l}^{+}+\dots+\kappa_{M}^{+}\right)\right)^{2}\left|z_{l}^{+}-z_{l-1}^{+}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}\ (f\star\nu)_{t},

where the last step uses Lemma
3.2
and

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[|r_{nk}|]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}n^{-3/4}\sum_{k=1}^{n}k^{-1/2}\longrightarrow 0.

∎

We have to prove

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(y_{nk}-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]\right)\left(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}0.

As the conditional expectation given
\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}
is
\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}
-measurable, and the increment
W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]\left(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[y_{nk}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}]\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right]=0

and it suffices to evaluate

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[y_{nk}\left(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[y_{nk}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M,z_{m}^{-},\dots,z_{M}^{+})\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{n^{1/4}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)+\sqrt{|r_{nk}|}\right),

where the first inequality is due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for conditional expectation and the second one uses (
I.5
) together with the inequality
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for
a,b\geq 0
. By Corollary
B.2
,

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\frac{1}{n^{1/4}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)/n}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{4\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}/n}\right)\right]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{1}{n^{3/4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}n^{-1/4}.

Moreover, by Jensen’s inequality for sums,

\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sqrt{|r_{nk}|}\right)^{2}=n\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sqrt{|r_{nk}|}\right)^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}|r_{nk}|.

By the moment bound (
I.6
), we then deduce
n^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\sqrt{|r_{nk}|}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}0
and finally get

\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[y_{nk}\left(W_{k/n}-W_{(k-1)/n}\right)|\mathcal{F}_{(k-1)/n}\right]\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}}0.

∎

First, we observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|y_{nk}\right|&=\left|\sum_{l=1}^{m}\kappa_{l}^{-}\left(-\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)+\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\left.\sum_{l=1}^{M}\kappa_{l}^{+}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{9}Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)+\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)\right)\right|\\
&\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|\kappa_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{1}^{-}|,|\kappa_{1}^{+}|,\dots,|\kappa_{M}^{+}|\}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\left(1+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|\right).\end{split}

(I.7)

Now let
\epsilon>0
and choose

a>2\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}|\kappa_{l}^{-}|+\sum_{l=1}^{M}|\kappa_{l}^{+}|\right)\max\left\{\left|\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right|,\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right|\right\}+\epsilon.

Then,

\displaystyle\left\{|y_{nk}|>a\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{m}|\kappa_{l}^{-}|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|+\left|\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\right)\right|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{z_{l}^{-}/n,0}}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt+\left.\sum_{l=1}^{M}|\kappa_{l}^{+}|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|+\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z_{l}^{+}/n}}\right)\right)>a\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|\kappa_{l}^{-}||Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|\kappa_{l}^{+}||Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|>\epsilon\right\}

and in particular

\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y_{nk}|>a\}}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|\kappa_{l}^{-}||Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|\kappa_{l}^{+}||Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|\right)^{2}.

Together with (
I.7
) and Proposition
3.4
we then find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|y_{nk}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y_{nk}|>a\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|\kappa_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{1}^{-}|,|\kappa_{1}^{+}|,\dots,|\kappa_{M}^{+}|\}^{4}\epsilon^{-2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\left.\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|\kappa_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{1}^{-}|,|\kappa_{1}^{+}|,\dots,|\kappa_{M}^{+}|\}^{4}\epsilon^{-2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|^{2}\right]+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|^{2}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\left.\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{l}^{-}/n,0)|^{4}\right]+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|Z_{k}^{j}(0,z_{l}^{+}/n)|^{4}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}(m,M)\max\{|\kappa_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|\kappa_{1}^{-}|,|\kappa_{1}^{+}|,\dots,|\kappa_{M}^{+}|\}^{4}\epsilon^{-2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\left(\frac{\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\max\{|z_{m}^{-}|,\dots,|z_{M}^{+}|\}^{4}}{n^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}.

Then, summing over
k
yields for some constant
C
depending on
\alpha,\beta,m,M
and all
\kappa_{l}^{\pm}
,
z_{l}^{\pm}

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}|y_{nk}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y_{nk}|>a\}}\right]\leq C\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor nt\rfloor}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq\frac{C\sqrt{nt}}{n}\longrightarrow 0.

∎

Lemma I.1
.

Let
K>0
. Then the sequence
(\ell_{n}(\cdot/n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of processes
(\ell_{n}(z/n))_{z\in[-K,K]}
is tight in
\mathcal{D}([-K,K])
.

We prove tightness of
(\ell_{n}(\cdot/n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
by verifying the moment condition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|\ell_{n}(z_{1}/n)-\ell_{n}(z_{2}/n)||\ell_{n}(z_{2}/n)-\ell_{n}(z_{3}/n)|\right]\leq C|z_{1}-z_{3}|^{\gamma}

(I.8)

for
z_{1}\leq z_{2}\leq z_{3}
, some constant
C>0
and
\gamma>1
(see
Billingsley (1999)
, Remark
(13.14)
after Theorem
13.5
). Recall the random variables
Z_{k}^{j}(\theta^{\prime},\theta)
given in Section
3
in (
3.9
) and (
3.10
). With those, we obtain for
a<b
,
a,b\in\{1,2,3\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\ell_{n}(z_{a}/n)-\ell_{n}(z_{b}/n)|&\leq\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{a}/n\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{b}/n\}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{a}/n\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{b}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\left|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{a}/n,z_{b}/n)\right|.\end{split}

(I.9)

Next, we will find an upper bound of the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}})
-norm of the last of these three summands. By Proposition
3.4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\left|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{a}/n,z_{b}/n)\right|\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq 2^{8}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{a}/n,z_{b}/n)\right|\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq 2^{8}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{i}/n,z_{j}/n)\right|^{2}\right]+2^{9}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\left|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{a}/n,z_{b}/n)Z_{l}^{j}(z_{a}/n,z_{b}/n)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{|z_{a}-z_{b}|^{2}}{n}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k(l-k)}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}|z_{a}-z_{b}|^{2}.

Defining

B_{n}(z_{a},z_{b}):=|z_{a}-z_{b}|^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{9}\left|Z_{k}^{j}(z_{a}/n,z_{b}/n)\right|,

the preceding estimate gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}[|B_{n}(z_{a},z_{b})|^{2}]\leq C_{\alpha,\beta},

(I.10)

with a bound
C_{\alpha,\beta}>0
independent of
n
and
z_{a},z_{b}
, and we deduce from (
I.9
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\ell_{n}(z_{a}/n)-\ell_{n}(z_{b}/n)|\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq|z_{a}-z_{b}|B_{n}(z_{a},z_{b})+\left|\log\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\right|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{a}/n\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{b}/n\}}\\
&\hskip 184.9429pt+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+z_{a}/n\leq X_{k/n}\leq\rho_{0}+z_{b}/n\leq X_{(k-1)/n}\}}\Big).\end{split}

(I.11)

We now show, how (
I.8
) follows from inequality (
I.11
) and the moment bound (
I.10
) for
B_{n}
. By (
I.9
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[|\ell_{n}(z_{1}/n)-\ell_{n}(z_{2}/n)||\ell_{n}(z_{2}/n)-\ell_{n}(z_{3}/n)|\right]
